<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the rational Hodge conjecture for complex abelian varieties with complex multiplication: every rational Hodge class on such a variety is a rational linear combination of algebraic cycle classes. As consequences, we obtain the generalized Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties, the Tate conjecture for abelian varieties over finite fields, and the Hodge standard conjecture for abelian varieties in arbitrary characteristic.

- **claim-cm-hodge-algebraicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cm)): Let $A$ be a complex CM abelian variety. For every integer $p\geq0$, the Betti cycle-class map $$\operatorname{cl}_B:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^p(A)_{\mathbb{Q}} \longrightarrow H^{2p}(A,\mathbb{Q})\cap H^{p,p}(A)$$ is surjective, where $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^p(A)_{\mathbb{Q}}=\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^p(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$ and the intersection is taken in $H^{2p}(A,\mathbb{C})$. In particular, the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on every finite product of complex CM abelian varieties and on every power of such a product.
  - Explanation: Every rational Hodge class on a complex CM abelian variety has a rational algebraic-cycle representative. The assertion also covers finite products and their powers.
- **claim-scalar-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-descent)): Let $T$ be a finite-dimensional rational vector space and $P\subset T_{\mathbb{C}}$ a complex-linear subspace. If $t\in T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}$ and $\sigma t\in P$ for every $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})$, then $$t\in (T\cap P)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}.$$ The same assertion applies to the subspace of maps of any fixed Hodge type in a Hom space.
  - Explanation: An algebraic-coefficient vector whose scalar conjugates all lie in a complex-linear subspace belongs to the scalar extension of its rational intersection. The same argument applies to maps of a fixed Hodge type.
- **claim-degree-one-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-one-maps)): For complex abelian varieties $A$ and $B$, pullback induces an isomorphism $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(A,B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q} \ \simeq\ \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\bigl(H^1(B,\mathbb{Q}),H^1(A,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$ If $S$ is a smooth connected projective complex variety and $h:H^1(B,\mathbb{Q})\to H^1(S,\mathbb{Q})$ is a rational Hodge map, then there are an integer $m\geq1$ and a morphism $f:S\to B$ such that $f^*=mh$ on $H^1(B,\mathbb{Q})$.
  - Explanation: Rational Hodge maps between degree-one cohomologies of abelian varieties are rational pullbacks of homomorphisms. A map into the cohomology of a smooth connected projective variety is realized by a morphism after clearing a denominator.
- **claim-toric-cm-characterization** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-toric-cm)): For a complex abelian variety $A$, the following conditions are equivalent:* 1. *$J(H^1(A,\mathbb{Q}))$ is a torus.* 2. *$H^1(A,\mathbb{Q})$ is a direct sum of rational Hodge structures, each of rank one over a CM field acting by Hodge endomorphisms.* 3. *$\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple algebra of rational dimension $2\dim A$.
  - Explanation: The CM condition is equivalent to a toric Hodge-circle group and to a decomposition of degree-one cohomology into rank-one CM-field modules.
- **claim-embedding-line-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cm-line-transfer)): Let $H$ and $H'$ be direct sums of rank-one weight-one Hodge modules over CM fields contained in $E$. Choose an embedding line $L\subset H_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}$ and an embedding line $M\subset H'_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}$ with the same complete type function. Extend any $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$-linear map $T:L\to M$ by zero on all other source embedding lines. Then $$T\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(H,H')\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}.$$ When $H$ and $H'$ are degree-one cohomologies of abelian varieties, this map is therefore an algebraic-coefficient linear combination of pullbacks of abelian morphisms, up to rational denominators.
  - Explanation: Matching complete type functions permit an arbitrary map between selected embedding lines to descend into the algebraic-coefficient span of rational Hodge maps. For abelian cohomology these maps are realized by pullbacks.
- **claim-four-factor-switch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-switch)): Let $E$ be a Galois CM field and let $v_1,v_2,v_3,v_4:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd sign functions satisfying $v_1+v_2=v_3+v_4$. Under the Künneth inclusion, the following line is algebraic: $$\label{eq:switch-line} U(v_1)_1\otimes U(v_2)_1\otimes U(v_3)_c\otimes U(v_4)_c \ \subset\ H^4\!\left(\prod_{i=1}^4B(v_i),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\right)$$
  - Explanation: The balanced sign relation makes the specified tensor line algebraic on the auxiliary product of CM abelian varieties.
- **claim-switch-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-switch-reduction)): If Theorem <a href="#thm:switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:switch">6</a> holds for every Galois CM field and every quadruple of odd sign functions satisfying its relation, then Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Universal algebraicity of switch lines implies the CM Hodge theorem through auxiliary factors, algebraic polarization contractions, and descent on the original variety.
- **claim-switch-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-switch-normalization)): It suffices to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:switch">6</a> when $s\geq2$ and $v_i(1)=1$ for all four functions.
  - Explanation: The switch problem reduces to types positive at the distinguished embedding and to the nontrivial totally real degree. The excluded cases reduce to algebraic pairs.
- **claim-surface-period-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-surface-switch)): Let $E$ be a Galois CM field with conjugation $c$, and let $v_1,v_2,v_3,v_4:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd sign functions satisfying $v_1+v_2=v_3+v_4$. Put $\lambda=(1,1,c,c)$. Suppose that $S$ is a smooth connected projective complex surface and that there are rational Hodge maps $$h_i:U(v_i)\longrightarrow H^1(S,\mathbb{Q}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq4,$$ and nonzero vectors $\alpha_i\in U(v_i)_{\lambda_i}\subset U(v_i)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}$ such that $$\label{eq:surface-period} \int_S h_1(\alpha_1)\smile h_2(\alpha_2) \smile h_3(\alpha_3)\smile h_4(\alpha_4)\ne0.$$ Here each $h_i$ is extended to $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$-coefficients. Then the line in Equation <a href="#eq:switch-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:switch-line">[eq:switch-line]</a> is algebraic.
  - Explanation: A nonzero surface period of rational CM Hodge images supplies a nonzero functional on the switch line. Algebraic divisor kernels convert that functional into an algebraic vector on the required line.
- **claim-complex-span-surface-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-surface-span)): Let $E$, $v_1,\ldots,v_4$, and $\lambda=(1,1,c,c)$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:surface-switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:surface-switch">9</a>, and let $S$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface. Suppose that, for each $1\leq i\leq4$, a class $\eta_i\in H^1(S,\mathbb{C})$ belongs to $$\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}} \{h(U(v_i)_{\lambda_i}): h\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(U(v_i),H^1(S,\mathbb{Q}))\}$$ and that $$\int_S\eta_1\smile\eta_2\smile\eta_3\smile\eta_4\ne0.$$ Then the line in Equation <a href="#eq:switch-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:switch-line">[eq:switch-line]</a> is algebraic.
  - Explanation: The surface criterion remains valid when the period factors lie in complex spans of rational Hodge images. Expanding finite sums selects a nonzero rational-map term.
- **claim-positive-lagrangian-half-determinant** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-positive-lagrangian)): The Hermitian forms on the lines $W_i$ can be chosen over $E/F$ so that $\mathcal F_P$ is a holomorphic positive Lagrangian for $\psi_P$, equivariantly for $G(F\otimes_\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{R})$. Its determinant and an equivariant square root have the form $$\label{eq:theta-halfdet} \det\mathcal F_P\simeq L^{2n}\otimes C_P,\qquad (\det\mathcal F_P)^{1/2}\simeq L^n\otimes C_P^{1/2},$$ where $C_P$ is a constant complex line on which $G(F\otimes_\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{R})$ acts trivially.
  - Explanation: Appropriate Hermitian line forms make the prescribed subbundle holomorphic, positive, and Lagrangian. Its determinant admits the specified equivariant square root, with a constant factor trivial for the special-unitary action.
- **claim-completed-theta-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-tensor-law)): Let $g\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb V_P)(F)$. At each real place write $$g=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\C&D_0\end{pmatrix},\qquad J=CZ+D_0,\qquad gZ=(AZ+B)J^{-1}.$$ Choose an infinite metaplectic lift with factor $j(g,Z)$ satisfying $j(g,Z)^2=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma}\det J_\tau$, and let $\widetilde g_f$ be its paired finite lift. Then $$\label{eq:theta-jacobi-law} \vartheta_{\omega_f(\widetilde g_f)\phi}(gZ,J^{-t}z) =j(g,Z)\exp\!\left(\pi i\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma} z_\tau^tJ_\tau^{-1}C_\tau z_\tau\right) \vartheta_\phi(Z,z).$$ The completed coefficients satisfy $$\label{eq:theta-taylor-law} T_{k,\omega_f(\widetilde g_f)\phi}(gZ) =j(g,Z)\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^k(J)\,T_{k,\phi}(Z).$$ They are thus tensors valued in $$(\det\mathcal F_P)^{1/2}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^k\mathcal F_P.$$ In particular the positive Lagrangian, not its dual, occurs in the symmetric factor. The tensor $T_{1,\phi}$ is holomorphic.
  - Explanation: Completion cancels the quadratic Jacobi factor and makes Taylor coefficients transform in symmetric powers of the positive Lagrangian. The first tensor is holomorphic.
- **claim-special-unitary-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-unitary-splitting)): For the symplectic space $(\mathbb V_P,\psi_P)$, restriction of the unitary splitting to $G(\mathbb{A}_F)$ yields an honest finite-adelic Weil action $\omega_{P,f}$. It is compatible with the rational covariance in Lemma <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a> and is independent of the splitting characters. At infinity its half-determinant action is the natural action on $L^n$ in <a href="#eq:theta-halfdet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-halfdet">[eq:theta-halfdet]</a>.* *At a split finite place, choose as Schrödinger positions for a multiplicity line one standard three-dimensional embedding summand. The resulting $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$-action is inverse linear substitution. More generally, the normalized linear action associated with a transformation $h$ of the position space is $$\label{eq:theta-linear-action} (\rho(h)b)(x)=\|\det h\|^{-1/2}b(h^{-1}x).$$ A bare metaplectic lift outside $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$ is the normalized operator $\rho(h)$ times its lift scalar; that scalar must be retained in any use of such a lift.* *Every finite list of inputs is fixed under $\omega_{P,f}$ by all sufficiently deep principal lattice congruence subgroups.
  - Explanation: Restricting the unitary splitting gives an honest smooth finite-adelic action compatible with rational covariance. Determinant twists vanish on the special-unitary group, and finite input families acquire common principal stabilizers.
- **claim-theta-cotangent-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-theta-one)): For every finite input $\phi$, the form $\Theta_i(\phi)$ is holomorphic on $D$ and descends to every sufficiently deep principal congruence quotient. At any fixed point of $D$, the values $\Theta_i(\phi)$, as $\phi$ varies over finite Schwartz inputs, span the cotangent fiber. Finitely many actual inputs already span that fiber and descend at a common sufficiently deep principal level.
  - Explanation: Theta one-forms are holomorphic and descend at sufficiently deep principal levels. Their values span every fixed cotangent fiber, with a finite actual input family sufficient for spanning.
- **claim-rank-two-wedge-and-continuity** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-theta-top)): The rank-two construction has the following properties.* 1. *At period data induced by a rational orthogonal decomposition $W=W_1\perp W_2$, the form $\Xi_W(\phi)$ is holomorphic. On a sufficiently deep common principal quotient it is a finite sum, with constant coefficients, of forms $\Theta_1(\phi_{1,r})\wedge\Theta_2(\phi_{2,r})$.* 2. *There is an input which is a product in adapted rational coordinates and for which $\Xi_W(\phi)$ is nonzero.* 3. *Fix $W$, the total finite input $\phi$, and a torsion-free principal quotient $X$ whose finite level fixes $\phi$ under $\omega_{W,f}$. With locally smooth constant-line trivializations, the map $$t\longmapsto\Xi_W(\phi;t)$$ is continuous in the $C^\infty$ topology of differential forms on this fixed compact quotient. The same finite level works throughout the parameter neighborhood.
  - Explanation: At rational decompositions the projected rank-two tensor is a finite wedge sum and is holomorphic. A product input makes it nonzero. Fixed total input and geometry permit smooth-topology continuity on a fixed compact quotient.
- **claim-fixed-input-switch-continuity** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity)): Suppose $d_1+d_2=d_3+d_4$, with $d_i(\mu)=1$ for all $i$. Start with $W=W_1\perp W_2$ as above, let $t_0$ be its compact-place data, and choose a total input $\phi$ for which $\xi=\Xi_W(\phi;t_0)$ is a nonzero holomorphic top form on a sufficiently deep compact principal quotient $X$. There are rational orthogonal decompositions $W=W_3\perp W_4$ with line signs $d_3,d_4$ whose total compact-place positive Lagrangians are arbitrarily close to those at $t_0$. The total active positive Lagrangian is exactly unchanged.* *For a sufficiently close rational choice $t'$, the form $\xi'=\Xi_W(\phi;t')$ is holomorphic on the same quotient $X$, with the same total finite input, and $$\label{eq:theta-nonzero-pairing} \int_X\xi\wedge\overline{\xi'}\ne0.$$ Both endpoints admit the wedge expansions of Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-top">16</a> on a further common principal cover, and their pairing remains nonzero there.
  - Explanation: Weak approximation supplies rational decompositions with the new signs near the original compact-place data. The active total Lagrangian and finite input remain fixed, preserving a nonzero mixed period and its pullback to a common cover.
- **claim-degree-one-cm-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cm-extraction)): Let $v:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd with $v(1)=1$, and use the multiplicity-line theta construction prescribed by $d(\tau)=v(\tau^{-1})$. Given a finite collection of its finite adelic Schwartz inputs, there is a common sufficiently deep principal ball quotient $S_0=\Gamma\backslash D$ on which every associated theta one-form class belongs to $$\label{eq:cm-extraction-span} \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{ f\bigl(U(v)_1\bigr): f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\bigl(U(v),H^1(S_0,\mathbb{Q})\bigr) \right\}.$$ The maps in this formula are rational Hodge maps, and their images are viewed after extension to $\mathbb{C}$. The conclusion persists after passage to a deeper principal level.
  - Explanation: A finite family of theta classes lies in complex spans of images of the prescribed CM embedding line under rational Hodge maps on a common principal quotient. Pullback preserves the conclusion.
- **claim-projective-reference-moduli** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-moduli)): The scheme $S$ is of finite type, smooth, and projective over $K_0$. Its complex connected components are compact ball quotients. For sufficiently divisible $N$, the component arising from $\Lambda$ is the quotient of $D$ by its principal special-unitary level.
  - Explanation: The full reference tuple scheme is smooth and projective with compact ball-quotient components. A sufficiently divisible marking level identifies the prescribed component with its principal special-unitary quotient.
- **claim-good-models-and-ordinary-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes)): After one finite extension of $K$ and removal of finitely many primes, the $S_j$ have smooth proper models with geometrically connected fibers, the $A_j$ extend to abelian schemes, and the $a_j$ extend to the models. The model of $S$ lies in the full tuple parameter scheme, retaining the $\mathcal O_E$-action and its graph coordinates. Differences of Albanese images generate every geometric fiber of each $A_j$.* *At every remaining rational prime $q$ completely split in $K$, the ordinary locus of the universal abelian scheme is dense in each geometric component of the special fiber of $S$.
  - Explanation: After finite arithmetic exclusions, the moduli components, Albanese varieties, and maps extend with fiberwise generation by Albanese differences. Ordinary points are dense on every component at remaining completely split rational primes.
- **claim-algebraic-hecke-albanese-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-hecke-albanese)): The rational spherical Hecke algebra of $(\mathscr G_q,\mathscr K_q)$ acts in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(A_S)\otimes\mathbb{Q}$ and, by pullback, on $H^1(S,\mathbb{Q})$. A basis operator in the positive range <a href="#eq:positive-hecke-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-hecke-range">[eq:positive-hecke-range]</a> is an integral homomorphism defined over $K$. Its pullback on one-forms is the sum of pullbacks over its target branches.
  - Explanation: The raw rational spherical Hecke algebra acts through algebraic Albanese endomorphisms. Positive-range basis positions give integral maps, whose action on one-forms is the unnormalized branch sum.
- **claim-integral-frobenius-hecke-polynomial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-albanese-congruence)): In the integral endomorphism ring of $\widetilde A_S$, $$\label{eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial} \Phi^3-D_1\Phi^2+qD_2\Phi-q^3D_3=0.$$
  - Explanation: Ordinary-neighbor multiplicities yield the cubic Frobenius relation in the integral endomorphism ring of the reduced Albanese product.
- **claim-active-inverse-hecke-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta)): Every double coset in $\mathcal H_w^0$ has all its branches within each component of $S$. On the original ball component, its pullback action on theta classes is the inverse spherical action on the active local Weil input: a local double coset acts through its inverse.
  - Explanation: Active determinant-zero Hecke branches stay inside each component. On theta classes their geometric pullback corresponds to the inverse local spherical action.
- **claim-radial-scalar-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation)): Fix the input at all finite places other than $w$. There are $M>0$, an element $r\in E^\times$, and a nonzero algebraic number $\omega$ such that $$r\bar r=1,\qquad \operatorname{val}_{w}(r)=M,\qquad \operatorname{val}_{\bar w}(r)=-M,$$ with zero valuation at every other finite prime, and the map from radial inputs to theta one-form classes factors through $\mathcal D_q^M=\omega$. In the covariance convention of Lemma <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a>, $$\label{eq:radial-scalar-weight} \omega^2=\zeta\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma}\tau(r)^{-3d(\tau)}$$ for a root of unity $\zeta$. Every root of $X^M-\omega$ has absolute value one under every complex embedding.
  - Explanation: A rational norm-one scalar produces a finite algebraic dilation relation on the theta-class image of radial inputs. Its infinite-place weights determine the squared eigenvalue, and every complex conjugate of each dilation root has unit modulus.
- **claim-active-satake-parameter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-theta-satake)): Every active spherical character occurring in a theta class of the multiplicity line $P$ has half-modulus projective parameter $$\label{eq:active-satake-triple} \{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e\},$$ where $e=e_0^{-1}$ for a root $e_0^M=\omega$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-radial-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-radial-relation">24</a>. It is algebraic and all its complex conjugates have modulus one. For every $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})$, $$\label{eq:active-conjugate-valuations} \nu_\iota(\sigma e) =-\frac32d\bigl((\sigma\|_E)^{-1}\bigr) =-\frac32v(\sigma\|_E).$$ A scalar-conjugate active character has a representative of the form <a href="#eq:active-satake-triple" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-satake-triple">[eq:active-satake-triple]</a> with third entry $e'=\pm\sigma e$.
  - Explanation: The inverse spherical action determines the active projective Satake triple. Scalar covariance supplies algebraicity, unit complex moduli, and the conjugate valuation prescribed by the CM sign function.
- **claim-central-hecke-finite-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-central-finite-order)): The central operators $T_3$ and $C_0$ have finite order on the full tuple space $S$ and on $A_S$.
  - Explanation: Powers of the central lattice positions are identified with the original full tuples by field scalars and a marking-preserving power. The induced Albanese operators therefore have finite order.
- **claim-filtered-pure-slope-summands** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands)): Let $\mathcal A/\mathbb{Z}_q$ be an abelian scheme with generic fiber $A$, and put $$D_A=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(A/\mathbb{Q}_q).$$ Equip $D_A$ with its Hodge filtration and its good-reduction crystalline Frobenius $\varphi_A$. Then $D_A$ is weakly admissible, its filtration has only indices $0$ and $1$, and endomorphisms extending over $\mathbb{Z}_q$ preserve the filtration and commute with $\varphi_A$.* *Let $L/\mathbb{Q}_q$ be finite. Suppose that an $L$-linear idempotent $p$ on $D_A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_q}L$ preserves the filtration and commutes with $\varphi_A\otimes1$. If every Frobenius slope on its image is $t\in\{0,1\}$, then the induced filtration satisfies $$F^1\bigl(p(D_A\otimes L)\bigr)= \begin{cases} 0,&t=0,\\ p(D_A\otimes L),&t=1. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: Extending endomorphisms preserve the degree-one filtration and Frobenius. A filtration-preserving Frobenius-commuting idempotent with a pure extreme slope has the corresponding pure induced filtration, because both complementary summands satisfy weak-admissibility inequalities.
- **claim-active-pure-hodge-types** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-active-hodge-types)): Let $\eta$ be an active numerical character with projective parameter $\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e'\}$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-satake" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-satake">25</a> or by scalar conjugation, and put $$t_\eta=\frac12-\frac13\nu_\iota(e')\in\{0,1\}.$$ For every full numerical character $\chi$ extending $\eta$, the space $H_\chi\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}$ is contained in $H^{1,0}(A_S)$ if $t_\eta=1$, and in $H^{0,1}(A_S)$ if $t_\eta=0$. Consequently $H^{\mathrm{act}}_\eta\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}$ has the same pure Hodge type.
  - Explanation: Weil weight selects the middle Frobenius candidate on every full generalized Hecke summand. The central relation fixes its slope, and filtration-preserving projectors turn that slope into the specified pure Hodge type without assuming semisimplicity.
- **claim-finite-family-common-cm-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction)): Fix $E,V,D,\Lambda$ and the principal lattice levels of Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">5</a>. Let $v_1,\ldots,v_m:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd with $v_j(1)=1$. For each $j$, choose a finite family of finite adelic Schwartz inputs in the multiplicity-line construction $d_j(\tau)=v_j(\tau^{-1})$. Let $X_{\mathrm{base}}$ be a principal quotient at a torsion-free level in this framework. There is a common deeper principal cover $Y\to X_{\mathrm{base}}$, with $Y$ a smooth connected projective surface, on which every associated theta one-form class from the $j$-th family belongs to $$\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{ f\bigl(U(v_j)_1\bigr): f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\bigl(U(v_j),H^1(Y,\mathbb{Q})\bigr) \right\}.$$ The maps are rational Hodge maps, with their images viewed after extension to $\mathbb{C}$. The conclusion persists after pullback to every deeper principal level. The auxiliary primes used for different $v_j$ may differ.
  - Explanation: Finite theta families of different normalized CM types admit all their rational-map span conclusions on one smooth connected projective principal cover. Each family may use its own auxiliary prime.
- **claim-effective-simple-cm-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-cc-realization)): Let $W$ be a nonzero simple polarizable rational Hodge structure of CM type and weight $k$. Put $$r_0=\min\{p:W^{p,k-p}\ne0\},\qquad n=k-2r_0.$$ Then $n\ge0$, and there is a CM abelian variety $B$, possibly a point, and a surjective rational Hodge map $$H^n(B,\mathbb{Q})(-r_0)\longrightarrow W.$$
  - Explanation: An effective twist of a simple polarizable CM Hodge structure is a quotient of cohomology of an auxiliary CM abelian variety in the matching weight. The zero-weight case permits a point.
- **claim-generalized-cm-hodge-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-ghc)): Let $A$ be a complex CM abelian variety. For integers $k,r\ge0$ and a rational Hodge substructure $W\subset H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})$ such that $W(r)$ is effective, there is a closed algebraic subset $Z\subset A$ of codimension at least $r$ such that $$W\subset\ker\bigl(H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow H^k(A\setminus Z,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$ This includes every finite product of CM abelian varieties and every power of such a product.
  - Explanation: An effective twist of a rational cohomology substructure of a CM abelian variety has algebraic support of the predicted codimension. Auxiliary effective realizations and algebraized correspondences supply the support.
- **claim-finite-field-tate-algebraicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-tate)): For every abelian variety $A/\mathbb{F}_q$, every integer $r\ge0$, and every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F}_q)$, the cycle-class map $$\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^r(A)\otimes_\mathbb{Z}\mathbb{Q}_\ell\longrightarrow H^{2r}_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\bar A,\mathbb{Q}_\ell(r))^{G_q}$$ is surjective. Numerical equivalence and $\ell$-adic homological equivalence coincide on rational algebraic cycles on $\bar A$.
  - Explanation: The CM Hodge theorem and the cited motivic implication give algebraic spanning of Tate classes. Averaged proper pushforward descends invariant classes to cycles over the original finite field, and numerical and homological equivalence coincide.
- **claim-abelian-hodge-standard-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-standard)): Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field and $A/K$ an abelian variety of dimension $g$. For every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(K)$, numerical and $\ell$-adic homological equivalence coincide on rational algebraic cycles on $A$. For every rational ample divisor class $h$ and every integer $0\le r\le g/2$, the rational symmetric form $$P_h^r(A)\times P_h^r(A)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q},\qquad (x,y)\longmapsto(-1)^r\deg(xy\,h^{g-2r})$$ is positive definite after extension of scalars to $\mathbb{R}$. Thus the Hodge standard conjecture for $\ell$-adic étale cohomology holds for abelian varieties in arbitrary characteristic.
  - Explanation: For abelian varieties over algebraically closed fields, numerical and admissible étale homological equivalence coincide. The signed intersection form on primitive rational cycles is positive definite for every rational ample divisor class in the stated codimension range.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-toric-cm",
        "exact_quote": "For a complex abelian variety $A$, the following conditions are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$J(H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q}))$ is a torus.*\n\n2.  *$H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q})$ is a direct sum of rational Hodge structures, each of rank one over a CM field acting by Hodge endomorphisms.*\n\n3.  *$\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(A)\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Z}}\\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple algebra of rational dimension $2\\dim A$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-toric-cm",
          "exact_quote": "For a complex abelian variety $A$, the following conditions are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$J(H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q}))$ is a torus.*\n\n2.  *$H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q})$ is a direct sum of rational Hodge structures, each of rank one over a CM field acting by Hodge endomorphisms.*\n\n3.  *$\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(A)\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Z}}\\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple algebra of rational dimension $2\\dim A$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a complex abelian variety $A$, the following conditions are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$J(H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q}))$ is a torus.*\n\n2.  *$H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q})$ is a direct sum of rational Hodge structures, each of rank one over a CM field acting by Hodge endomorphisms.*\n\n3.  *$\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(A)\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Z}}\\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple algebra of rational dimension $2\\dim A$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cm-line-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ and $H'$ be direct sums of rank-one weight-one Hodge modules over CM fields contained in $E$. Choose an embedding line $L\\subset H_{\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}}$ and an embedding line $M\\subset H'_{\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}}$ with the same complete type function. Extend any $\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}$-linear map $T:L\\to M$ by zero on all other source embedding lines. Then $$T\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}(H,H')\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Q}}\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}.$$ When $H$ and $H'$ are degree-one cohomologies of abelian varieties, this map is therefore an algebraic-coefficient linear combination of pullbacks of abelian morphisms, up to rational denominators.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-tensor-law",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}(\\mathbb V_P)(F)$. At each real place write $$g=\\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\\\C&D_0\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n J=CZ+D_0,\\qquad gZ=(AZ+B)J^{-1}.$$ Choose an infinite metaplectic lift with factor $j(g,Z)$ satisfying $j(g,Z)^2=\\prod_{\\tau\\in\\Sigma}\\det J_\\tau$, and let $\\widetilde g_f$ be its paired finite lift. Then $$\\label{eq:theta-jacobi-law}\n \\vartheta_{\\omega_f(\\widetilde g_f)\\phi}(gZ,J^{-t}z)\n  =j(g,Z)\\exp\\!\\left(\\pi i\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\Sigma}\n             z_\\tau^tJ_\\tau^{-1}C_\\tau z_\\tau\\right)\n             \\vartheta_\\phi(Z,z).$$ The completed coefficients satisfy $$\\label{eq:theta-taylor-law}\n T_{k,\\omega_f(\\widetilde g_f)\\phi}(gZ)\n   =j(g,Z)\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k(J)\\,T_{k,\\phi}(Z).$$ They are thus tensors valued in $$(\\det\\mathcal F_P)^{1/2}\\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k\\mathcal F_P.$$ In particular the positive Lagrangian, not its dual, occurs in the symmetric factor. The tensor $T_{1,\\phi}$ is holomorphic.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unitary-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "For the symplectic space $(\\mathbb V_P,\\psi_P)$, restriction of the unitary splitting to $G(\\mathbb{A}_F)$ yields an honest finite-adelic Weil action $\\omega_{P,f}$. It is compatible with the rational covariance in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:theta-tensor-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:theta-tensor-law\">12</a> and is independent of the splitting characters. At infinity its half-determinant action is the natural action on $L^n$ in <a href=\"#eq:theta-halfdet\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-halfdet\">[eq:theta-halfdet]</a>.*\n\n\n*At a split finite place, choose as Schrödinger positions for a multiplicity line one standard three-dimensional embedding summand. The resulting $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$-action is inverse linear substitution. More generally, the normalized linear action associated with a transformation $h$ of the position space is $$\\label{eq:theta-linear-action}\n (\\rho(h)b)(x)=|\\det h|^{-1/2}b(h^{-1}x).$$ A bare metaplectic lift outside $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$ is the normalized operator $\\rho(h)$ times its lift scalar; that scalar must be retained in any use of such a lift.*\n\n*Every finite list of inputs is fixed under $\\omega_{P,f}$ by all sufficiently deep principal lattice congruence subgroups.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-unitary-splitting",

The resulting $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$-action is inverse linear substitution. More generally, the normalized linear action associated with a transformation $h$ of the position space is $$\\label{eq:theta-linear-action}\n (\\rho(h)b)(x)=|\\det h|^{-1/2}b(h^{-1}x).$$ A bare metaplectic lift outside $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$ is the normalized operator $\\rho(h)$ times its lift scalar; that scalar must be retained in any use of such a lift.*\n\n*Every finite list of inputs is fixed under $\\omega_{P,f}$ by all sufficiently deep principal lattice congruence subgroups.",

        "exact_quote": "For every finite input $\\phi$, the form $\\Theta_i(\\phi)$ is holomorphic on $D$ and descends to every sufficiently deep principal congruence quotient.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-theta-top",
        "exact_quote": "The rank-two construction has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *At period data induced by a rational orthogonal decomposition $W=W_1\\perp W_2$, the form $\\Xi_W(\\phi)$ is holomorphic. On a sufficiently deep common principal quotient it is a finite sum, with constant coefficients, of forms $\\Theta_1(\\phi_{1,r})\\wedge\\Theta_2(\\phi_{2,r})$.*\n\n2.  *There is an input which is a product in adapted rational coordinates and for which $\\Xi_W(\\phi)$ is nonzero.*\n\n3.  *Fix $W$, the total finite input $\\phi$, and a torsion-free principal quotient $X$ whose finite level fixes $\\phi$ under $\\omega_{W,f}$. With locally smooth constant-line trivializations, the map $$t\\longmapsto\\Xi_W(\\phi;t)$$ is continuous in the $C^\\infty$ topology of differential forms on this fixed compact quotient. The same finite level works throughout the parameter neighborhood.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $d_1+d_2=d_3+d_4$, with $d_i(\\mu)=1$ for all $i$. Start with $W=W_1\\perp W_2$ as above, let $t_0$ be its compact-place data, and choose a total input $\\phi$ for which $\\xi=\\Xi_W(\\phi;t_0)$ is a nonzero holomorphic top form on a sufficiently deep compact principal quotient $X$. There are rational orthogonal decompositions $W=W_3\\perp W_4$ with line signs $d_3,d_4$ whose total compact-place positive Lagrangians are arbitrarily close to those at $t_0$. The total active positive Lagrangian is exactly unchanged.*\n\n\n*For a sufficiently close rational choice $t'$, the form $\\xi'=\\Xi_W(\\phi;t')$ is holomorphic on the same quotient $X$, with the same total finite input, and $$\\label{eq:theta-nonzero-pairing}\n \\int_X\\xi\\wedge\\overline{\\xi'}\\ne0.$$ Both endpoints admit the wedge expansions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:theta-top\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:theta-top\">16</a> on a further common principal cover, and their pairing remains nonzero there.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity",

There are rational orthogonal decompositions $W=W_3\\perp W_4$ with line signs $d_3,d_4$ whose total compact-place positive Lagrangians are arbitrarily close to those at $t_0$. The total active positive Lagrangian is exactly unchanged.*\n\n\n*For a sufficiently close rational choice $t'$, the form $\\xi'=\\Xi_W(\\phi;t')$ is holomorphic on the same quotient $X$, with the same total finite input, and $$\\label{eq:theta-nonzero-pairing}\n \\int_X\\xi\\wedge\\overline{\\xi'}\\ne0.$$ Both endpoints admit the wedge expansions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:theta-top\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:theta-top\">16</a> on a further common principal cover, and their pairing remains nonzero there.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $v:G_E\\to\\{1,-1\\}$ be odd with $v(1)=1$, and use the multiplicity-line theta construction prescribed by $d(\\tau)=v(\\tau^{-1})$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A/\\mathbb{Z}_q$ be an abelian scheme with generic fiber $A$, and put $$D_A=H^1_{\\mathrm{dR}}(A/\\mathbb{Q}_q).$$ Equip $D_A$ with its Hodge filtration and its good-reduction crystalline Frobenius $\\varphi_A$. Then $D_A$ is weakly admissible, its filtration has only indices $0$ and $1$, and endomorphisms extending over $\\mathbb{Z}_q$ preserve the filtration and commute with $\\varphi_A$.*\n\n*Let $L/\\mathbb{Q}_q$ be finite. Suppose that an $L$-linear idempotent $p$ on $D_A\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Q}_q}L$ preserves the filtration and commutes with $\\varphi_A\\otimes1$.         "source_id": "PM-032.1"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Complex CM abelian variety** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A complex abelian variety $A$ is of *CM type*, or simply *CM*, if $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra of dimension $2\dim A$.
- **Rational Hodge classes and rational cycle span** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, write $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{2p}(X)=H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{Q})\cap H^{p,p}(X),\qquad \mathcal Z^{2p}(X)= \mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^p(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$
- **Algebraic class after scalar extension** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A class with coefficients in $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ or $\mathbb{C}$ is called *algebraic* if it belongs to the corresponding scalar extension of $\mathcal Z^{2p}(X)$.
- **Tate twist convention** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Our Tate convention is $V(r)^{a,b}=V^{a+r,b+r}$, so $V(r)$ has weight $\operatorname{wt}(V)-2r$.
- **Weight-one Hodge-circle group** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a polarizable rational Hodge structure $H$ of weight one, let $J(H)$ be the smallest rational algebraic subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(H)$ containing the Hodge circle.
- **Odd sign function** ([source](#pm-h-8)): An *odd sign function* on $G_E$ is a function $v:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ satisfying $v(c\tau)=-v(\tau)$.
- **Rank-one CM Hodge structure and embedding line** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Give the rational vector space $E$ a weight-one Hodge structure $U(v)$ by declaring its embedding line $$U(v)_\lambda= \{u\in U(v)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}: au=\lambda(a)u\text{ for every }a\in E\} \qquad(\lambda\in G_E)$$ to have type $(1,0)$ if $v(\lambda)=1$, and type $(0,1)$ otherwise.
- **Complex ball of negative lines** ([source](#eq:theta-cotangent)): Put $$G=\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(V),\qquad D=\{\,L\subset V_\mu:\dim_\mathbb{C}L=1,\ h_V\|_L<0\,\}.$$
- **Multiplicity-space symplectic form** ([source](#eq:theta-symplectic-form)): The product of the two Hermitian forms is a well-defined Hermitian form on $V\otimes_E P$, and we use the $F$-valued form $$\label{eq:theta-symplectic-form} \psi_P(v\otimes p,v'\otimes p') =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/F}\!\bigl(\delta\,h_V(v,v')h_P(p,p')\bigr).$$
- **Completed theta series and Taylor tensors** ([source](#eq:theta-completion)): Define $$\label{eq:theta-completion} \begin{split} \widehat\vartheta_\phi(Z,z) &=\exp\!\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma} z_\tau^t(\operatorname{Im}Z_\tau)^{-1}z_\tau\right) \vartheta_\phi(Z,z),\\ \widehat\vartheta_\phi(Z,z) &=\sum_{k\ge0}\langle T_{k,\phi}(Z),z^{\otimes k}\rangle . \end{split}$$
- **Numerical Hecke character** ([source](#eq:active-generalized-space)): A numerical character is a $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra homomorphism $\chi:\mathbb T\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$.
- **Effective pure rational Hodge structure** ([source](#pm-h-30)): A pure rational Hodge structure is *effective* if all its nonzero Hodge components have nonnegative indices.
- **CM type for a polarizable Hodge structure** ([source](#pm-h-30)): A polarizable $V$ is *of CM type* if $J(V)$ is a torus, with the trivial group allowed.
- **Geometric coniveau filtration** ([source](#pm-h-30)): For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the geometric coniveau filtration is $$N^rH^k(X,\mathbb{Q})= \sum_{\substack{Z\subset X\text{ closed}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_X Z\ge r}} \ker\bigl(H^k(X,\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow H^k(X\setminus Z,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$
- **Rational ample divisor class** ([source](#pm-h-32)): A *rational ample divisor class* is an $h\in A^1_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}$ for which some positive integral multiple is the numerical class of an ample Cartier divisor.
- **Primitive numerical cycle space** ([source](#pm-h-32)): For integers $0\le r\le g/2$, put $$P_h^r(A)=\ker\!\left( h^{g-2r+1}:A^r_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q} \longrightarrow A^{g-r+1}_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}\right).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-cm-endomorphism-dimension | Rational dimension of the commutative semisimple algebra in the CM condition | = | $2\dim A$ | The dimension of the chosen abelian variety. |
| q-real-subfield-degree | Degree parameter of the maximal totally real subfield | = | $[F:\mathbb{Q}]$ | The field used to index the auxiliary CM types. |
| q-auxiliary-cm-dimension | Dimension of the auxiliary abelian variety attached to an odd sign function | = | $s$ | The chosen odd sign function and common field. |
| q-normalized-switch-degree | Totally real degree in the normalized nontrivial switch construction | ≥ | $2$ | The totally real field degree and identity-embedding signs. |
| q-intermediate-polarization-exponent | Ample-divisor exponent used to contract each intermediate degree-one pair | = | $s-1$ | Each intermediate auxiliary abelian factor has the field-degree dimension. |
| q-multiplicity-symplectic-dimension | Dimension over the totally real field of the multiplicity-space symplectic restriction | = | $6n$ | The allowed multiplicity dimensions in the theta construction. |
| q-schrodinger-position-dimension | Dimension of the Schrödinger position coordinates over the totally real field | = | $3n$ | The multiplicity-space dimension. |
| q-reference-abelian-dimension | Dimension of the abelian varieties in the reference moduli scheme | = | $3s$ | The common totally real field degree. |
| q-active-conjugate-valuation | Normalized valuation of a scalar-conjugated third active Satake entry | = | $-\frac32v(\sigma\|_E)$ | All scalar conjugates of an active parameter occurring in a theta class. |
| q-active-pure-slope | Pure Frobenius slope of a full generalized Hecke extension of the active character | = | $\frac12-\frac13\nu_\iota(e')$ | Every full extension of the active character, including generalized eigenspaces. |
| q-effective-realization-weight | Weight of the effective twist realized by auxiliary CM cohomology | = | $k-2r_0$ | Its weight and smallest nonzero Hodge index. |
| q-generalized-hodge-support-codimension | Codimension of an algebraic support for an effective-twist Hodge substructure | ≥ | $r$ | The nonnegative twist index in the generalized Hodge statement. |
| q-primitive-cycle-codimension-range | Upper codimension bound for the primitive positive intersection form | ≤ | $g/2$ | Nonnegative codimensions in the primitive range. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-scalar-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-descent): Take traces in a finite Galois coefficient field and recover rational components with a trace-dual basis. -> Membership in the scalar extension of the rational intersection.
- **step-degree-one-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-one-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-one-maps): Clear lattice denominators to obtain a complex-torus homomorphism, then use algebraicity and the Albanese map. -> An algebraic morphism realizing the rational Hodge map after multiplication.
- **step-toric-cm-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-lem-toric-cm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-toric-cm): Analyze the associative envelope of the Hodge torus using the positive polarization adjoint, then retain every rank-one field summand. -> Equivalent CM characterizations and a decomposition preserving multiplicities.
- **step-line-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-cm-line-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cm-line-transfer): Zero-extend the selected line map, check the Hodge type of every scalar conjugate, and descend in the rational Hom space. -> A line map in the algebraic-coefficient span of rational Hodge maps and, where applicable, morphism pullbacks.
- **step-normalize-switch** [claim](#stmt-lem-switch-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-switch-normalization): Separate the ordinary-pair case; otherwise translate to an embedding where all signs are positive and transfer the normalized lines back. -> Reduction to the normalized nontrivial switch.
- **step-surface-functional-to-tensor** [claim](#stmt-prop-surface-switch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-surface-switch): Realize the Hodge maps by morphisms, push forward the surface class, and contract it with pure algebraic divisor kernels. -> Algebraicity of the exact switch line.
- **step-complex-span-expansion** [claim](#stmt-cor-surface-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-surface-span): Expand each period factor in a finite complex linear combination of rational Hodge images and select a nonzero term. -> A rational-map period to which the surface criterion applies.
- **step-positive-lagrangian** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-positive-lagrangian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-positive-lagrangian): Choose line coefficients with the required archimedean signs, use annihilator subbundles for holomorphy, and compute the determinant characters. -> A positive holomorphic Lagrangian and its equivariant half-determinant line.
- **step-theta-covariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-tensor-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-tensor-law): Check the Gaussian transformation on symplectic generators, apply rational summation invariance, and cancel the Jacobi factor with the completion. -> Tensor covariance with the positive Lagrangian and holomorphy of the first tensor.
- **step-unitary-splitting-and-level** [claim](#stmt-lem-unitary-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unitary-splitting): Restrict the unitary circle splitting to the special-unitary group, compare its infinite lift, and use smooth finite stabilizers. -> An honest finite action and sufficiently deep common principal stabilizers.
- **step-cotangent-spanning** [claim](#stmt-prop-theta-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-theta-one): Use sparse lattice-coset inputs and Gaussian tail decay to obtain spanning limits, then select finitely many actual input values. -> Holomorphic theta one-forms spanning the cotangent fiber and descending together.
- **step-rank-two-wedge** [claim](#stmt-prop-theta-top) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-theta-top): Factor the input in adapted rational coordinates, apply double alternation to the quadratic product, and control derivatives on a fixed compact quotient. -> A nonzero holomorphic wedge sum at rational endpoints and continuity for fixed-data variation.
- **step-fixed-input-approximation** [claim](#stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity): Approximate prescribed compact-place lines rationally, keep the active total data fixed, and use continuity near a positive self-pairing. -> Rational endpoint forms with a nonzero mixed period on a common principal cover.
- **step-projective-reference-moduli** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-moduli) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-moduli): Bound action graphs in a finite-type parameter space, identify reduced ball charts, use anisotropy for compactness, and remove determinant roots with the marking level. -> A smooth projective tuple scheme with the prescribed principal ball component.
- **step-good-models-and-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes): Spread the full tuple data and Albanese generation maps; choose algebraic projector-split points whose split-prime reductions are ordinary. -> Good models, fiberwise Albanese generation, and ordinary density on every component.
- **step-algebraic-hecke** [claim](#stmt-lem-hecke-albanese) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hecke-albanese): Construct subgroup quotients with descended polarizations and markings, sum target Albanese maps, and extend positive positions by central clearing. -> Algebraic Hecke endomorphisms with integral positive-range operators.
- **step-ordinary-neighbor-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-prop-albanese-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-albanese-congruence): Count generic subgroup graph lifts and their special kernels, identify full-tuple Frobenius, and cancel the resulting multiset operations on Albanese differences. -> The integral cubic Frobenius–Hecke identity.
- **step-active-inverse-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta): Use strong approximation to identify active lattice branches within each component, then apply fixed-input rational theta covariance. -> Component preservation and the inverse raw spherical action on theta classes.
- **step-radial-scalar-relation** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation): Principalize an active prime power, form a norm-one scalar, enter the other-place stabilizers, and compute its bare rational theta weight. -> An algebraic dilation relation with controlled conjugate complex absolute values.
- **step-active-satake-computation** [claim](#stmt-prop-theta-satake) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-theta-satake): Diagonalize the radial dilation quotient, count minuscule lattice positions, invert the geometric parameter, and evaluate conjugate scalar valuations. -> The active projective parameter and its full scalar-conjugate valuation rule.
- **step-central-finite-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-central-finite-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-central-finite-order): Identify powered central target lattices with the source using field scalars and then take a further power fixing the marking. -> Finite-order central operators controlling the full Satake scalar.
- **step-filtered-summand-purity** [claim](#stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands): Use functorial good-reduction comparison and complementary filtration-preserving projectors to force equality of Hodge and Newton numbers on each summand. -> A pure induced Hodge filtration on the projected image.
- **step-active-hodge-types** [claim](#stmt-prop-active-hodge-types) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-active-hodge-types): Triangularize commuting operators, use Weil weight to eliminate incompatible Frobenius roots, and compare projected filtration ranks through fixed coefficient embeddings. -> Pure Hodge types for every full extension and its active generalized sum.
- **step-rational-cm-extraction** [claim](#stmt-thm-cm-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-active-hodge-types-2): Map the selected source line to arbitrary algebraic Betti vectors in an active summand, zero-extend, check all conjugate types, and descend the Hom space. -> Complex spans of rational CM Hodge images containing each theta class, stable under deeper pullback.
- **step-common-finite-family-cover** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction): Apply extraction to each finite family, intersect principal levels with the prescribed base level, and deepen to a projective reference component. -> One smooth connected projective cover carrying all required span conclusions.
- **step-assemble-switch** [claim](#stmt-thm-switch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction-2): Pull endpoint wedge expansions to the common projective cover, expand the nonzero mixed period, conjugate the last pair of rational source images, and apply the surface-span criterion. -> Algebraicity of every switch line.
- **step-balanced-tensor-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-switch-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-switch-reduction): Decompose supported exterior monomials, connect equal-sum sign lists by opposite-mismatch row swaps, compose algebraic transition tensors by polarization contractions, and return along the diagonal. -> Algebraicity of each supported original monomial and rational descent of its sum.
- **step-complete-cm-hodge** [claim](#stmt-thm-cm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction-3): Apply the tensor reduction after universal switch algebraicity and descend coefficients on the original CM variety. -> Surjectivity of the rational Betti cycle-class map in every codimension.
- **step-effective-cm-realization** [claim](#stmt-cc-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-realization): Twist to an effective structure, identify its simple toric envelope with a CM field, distribute embedding indices among weight-one CM types, and use field multiplication on a Künneth summand. -> A surjective rational Hodge map from matching auxiliary CM cohomology, with a point allowed in the zero-weight case.
- **step-generalized-hodge-support** [claim](#stmt-cc-ghc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-ghc): Reduce to simple summands, algebraize the auxiliary Hodge map by a correspondence on a CM product, and project its cycle support. -> A closed algebraic support of at least the required codimension.
- **step-finite-field-tate** [claim](#stmt-cc-tate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-tate): Apply the cited corrected CM-to-Tate implication, descend a finite family of geometric cycles to a finite field extension, and average its proper pushforward. -> A cycle class over the original finite field and equality of numerical and homological equivalence.
- **step-hodge-standard** [claim](#stmt-cc-standard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-standard): Apply the cited polarization implication with its corrections, identify numerical and homological primitive spaces, and rescale a rational ample class by a positive integral multiple. -> Positive definiteness of the primitive signed intersection form in arbitrary characteristic.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-one-maps",
          "exact_quote": "For complex abelian varieties $A$ and $B$, pullback induces an isomorphism $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(A,B)\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Z}}\\mathbb{Q}\n \\ \\simeq\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}\\bigl(H^1(B,\\mathbb{Q}),H^1(A,\\mathbb{Q})\\bigr).$$ If $S$ is a smooth connected projective complex variety and $h:H^1(B,\\mathbb{Q})\\to H^1(S,\\mathbb{Q})$ is a rational Hodge map, then there are an integer $m\\geq1$ and a morphism $f:S\\to B$ such that $f^*=mh$ on $H^1(B,\\mathbb{Q})$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.1"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cm-line-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ and $H'$ be direct sums of rank-one weight-one Hodge modules over CM fields contained in $E$. Choose an embedding line $L\\subset H_{\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}}$ and an embedding line $M\\subset H'_{\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}}$ with the same complete type function. Extend any $\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}$-linear map $T:L\\to M$ by zero on all other source embedding lines. Then $$T\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}(H,H')\\otimes_{\\mathbb{Q}}\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}.$$ When $H$ and $H'$ are degree-one cohomologies of abelian varieties, this map is therefore an algebraic-coefficient linear combination of pullbacks of abelian morphisms, up to rational denominators.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-surface-switch",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a Galois CM field with conjugation $c$, and let $v_1,v_2,v_3,v_4:G_E\\to\\{1,-1\\}$ be odd sign functions satisfying $v_1+v_2=v_3+v_4$. Put $\\lambda=(1,1,c,c)$. Suppose that $S$ is a smooth connected projective complex surface and that there are rational Hodge maps $$h_i:U(v_i)\\longrightarrow H^1(S,\\mathbb{Q}),\\qquad 1\\leq i\\leq4,$$ and nonzero vectors $\\alpha_i\\in U(v_i)_{\\lambda_i}\\subset U(v_i)_{\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}}$ such that $$\\label{eq:surface-period}\n \\int_S h_1(\\alpha_1)\\smile h_2(\\alpha_2)\n             \\smile h_3(\\alpha_3)\\smile h_4(\\alpha_4)\\ne0.$$ Here each $h_i$ is extended to $\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}$-coefficients. Then the line in Equation <a href=\"#eq:switch-line\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-line\">[eq:switch-line]</a> is algebraic.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-positive-lagrangian",
          "exact_quote": "The Hermitian forms on the lines $W_i$ can be chosen over $E/F$ so that $\\mathcal F_P$ is a holomorphic positive Lagrangian for $\\psi_P$, equivariantly for $G(F\\otimes_\\mathbb{Q}\\mathbb{R})$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-tensor-law",
          "exact_quote": "Let $g\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}(\\mathbb V_P)(F)$. At each real place write $$g=\\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\\\C&D_0\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n J=CZ+D_0,\\qquad gZ=(AZ+B)J^{-1}.$$ Choose an infinite metaplectic lift with factor $j(g,Z)$ satisfying $j(g,Z)^2=\\prod_{\\tau\\in\\Sigma}\\det J_\\tau$, and let $\\widetilde g_f$ be its paired finite lift. Then $$\\label{eq:theta-jacobi-law}\n \\vartheta_{\\omega_f(\\widetilde g_f)\\phi}(gZ,J^{-t}z)\n  =j(g,Z)\\exp\\!\\left(\\pi i\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\Sigma}\n             z_\\tau^tJ_\\tau^{-1}C_\\tau z_\\tau\\right)\n             \\vartheta_\\phi(Z,z).$$ The completed coefficients satisfy $$\\label{eq:theta-taylor-law}\n T_{k,\\omega_f(\\widetilde g_f)\\phi}(gZ)\n   =j(g,Z)\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k(J)\\,T_{k,\\phi}(Z).$$ They are thus tensors valued in $$(\\det\\mathcal F_P)^{1/2}\\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k\\mathcal F_P.$$ In particular the positive Lagrangian, not its dual, occurs in the symmetric factor. The tensor $T_{1,\\phi}$ is holomorphic.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-unitary-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "For the symplectic space $(\\mathbb V_P,\\psi_P)$, restriction of the unitary splitting to $G(\\mathbb{A}_F)$ yields an honest finite-adelic Weil action $\\omega_{P,f}$. It is compatible with the rational covariance in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:theta-tensor-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:theta-tensor-law\">12</a> and is independent of the splitting characters. At infinity its half-determinant action is the natural action on $L^n$ in <a href=\"#eq:theta-halfdet\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-halfdet\">[eq:theta-halfdet]</a>.*\n\n\n*At a split finite place, choose as Schrödinger positions for a multiplicity line one standard three-dimensional embedding summand. The resulting $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$-action is inverse linear substitution. More generally, the normalized linear action associated with a transformation $h$ of the position space is $$\\label{eq:theta-linear-action}\n (\\rho(h)b)(x)=|\\det h|^{-1/2}b(h^{-1}x).$$ A bare metaplectic lift outside $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_3$ is the normalized operator $\\rho(h)$ times its lift scalar; that scalar must be retained in any use of such a lift.*\n\n*Every finite list of inputs is fixed under $\\omega_{P,f}$ by all sufficiently deep principal lattice congruence subgroups.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-rational-cm-algebraicity**: The cycle-class surjectivity assertion concerns rational Hodge classes on complex CM abelian varieties, including their finite products and powers.
- **invariant-switch-balance**: The switch line is algebraic under the Galois CM and odd-sign balance hypotheses. Balance alone establishes its conjugate Hodge types; the geometric construction establishes algebraicity.
- **invariant-fixed-total-theta-data**: Continuity compares total theta forms while the rational Hermitian space, total finite input and compact quotient remain fixed. Endpoint factorization follows this comparison.
- **invariant-filtered-projector-condition**: A pure extreme Frobenius slope determines the induced Hodge filtration only for an idempotent that preserves the filtration and commutes with Frobenius.
- **invariant-common-projective-surface**: The surface criterion requires the prescribed rational Hodge sources and a nonzero period on the same smooth connected projective surface. Complex span membership suffices through finite expansion.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-non-cm-characteristic-zero**: Apply the main theorem to arbitrary complex abelian varieties. -> Special-fiber algebraicity does not establish characteristic-zero algebraicity for a non-CM variety.
- **anti-use-integral-cycle-surjectivity**: Replace rational cycle-class surjectivity by integral cycle-class surjectivity. -> The asserted coefficient regime allows rational denominators and supplies no integral surjectivity conclusion.
- **anti-use-fixed-power-ring-generation**: Generate the Hodge ring of a fixed power using only low-codimension classes on that power. -> The reduction introduces auxiliary factors and contracts intermediate slots before returning to the original variety.
- **anti-use-intermediate-holomorphy**: Treat every intermediate compact-place parameter as producing a holomorphic top form. -> The varying form is smooth and of the specified type, while holomorphy is established at rational decomposition endpoints.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-slope-projector**: Infer a pure Hodge type from a pure Frobenius slope without checking the projector. -> A slope projector can fail to preserve the Hodge filtration, invalidating the filtered summand argument.
- **anti-use-uniform-level-for-sparse-limit**: Assume the whole sparse-input sequence descends to a common principal level. -> Fiber spanning is proved using a limit, and a common level is chosen only after selecting finitely many actual inputs.
- **anti-use-double-cover-global-splitting**: Assume the metaplectic double cover splits on every adelic unitary group. -> The invoked unitary splitting concerns the circle extension; the comparison with a double lift requires restriction to the special-unitary group.
- **anti-use-tate-beyond-abelian-varieties**: Apply the finite-field cycle-class surjectivity conclusion to arbitrary projective varieties. -> The stated arithmetic consequence supplies an abelian-variety conclusion rather than a general finite-field theorem.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves algebraicity and rational-map span assertions for unrestricted CM varieties through infinite theta series, weak approximation, sufficiently deep principal levels and scalar descent. It does not provide a finite executable procedure producing cycle representatives from encoded input varieties and classes. The source explicitly separates a limiting argument from finite input selection: “The inputs $\phi_k$ in this limit need not have a common congruence stabilizer as $k$ varies.” The finite sign switches and ordinary-neighbor counts are auxiliary proof components; testing them would not certify the cycle-class surjectivity or the analytic and arithmetic extraction steps.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Surjectivity of the rational cycle-class map supplies a mathematical existence guarantee, but does not specify an executable procedure for producing cycle equations from a Hodge class. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nonzero mixed period concerns holomorphic forms on a compact arithmetic quotient; it supplies no game dynamics, simulation update rule, or physical evolution model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The CM extraction theorem places theta classes in spans of rational Hodge images, but does not provide a symbolic interface for computing those maps or selecting a usable principal level. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The tensor reduction contains a finite construction that transforms ordered lists of odd sign functions with equal sums into each other. Pairing opposite mismatches within a column gives local swaps that preserve oddness and leave previously corrected columns unchanged. This implements the combinatorial transition sequence, while algebraicity of its associated tensors remains dependent on the four-factor theorem. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Embedding-line decompositions and generalized Hecke spaces organize mathematical objects, but the paper establishes no storage, indexing, or query contract for them. In particular, generalized character spaces retain nilpotent parts rather than assuming a diagonal representation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theta construction uses infinite lattice sums and analytic convergence on compact parameter domains. It specifies neither an accelerator kernel nor a certified finite truncation suitable for hardware execution. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The algebraic spherical Hecke action is a correspondence construction on moduli tuples and Albanese varieties. It does not supply a cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cotangent-fiber spanning by theta inputs is a geometric spanning statement. It provides no learned representation, training objective, or statistical generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Weak approximation chooses rational decompositions sufficiently close to preserve a nonzero period. The construction does not optimize an objective or bound the arithmetic size of the chosen decomposition. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A common deeper principal cover places finite families of theta classes on the same projective surface. This geometric compatibility supplies no communication, replication, or distributed consistency protocol. |
| other (Algebraic and arithmetic geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The surface criterion constructs an algebraic tensor from a nonzero period: rational Hodge maps give morphisms into the CM factors, the pushed-forward surface supplies a functional, and algebraic divisor kernels convert it into a vector on the required embedding line. This is a mathematical construction suitable for adaptation into a symbolic or formal treatment once its geometric inputs are supplied. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-complex-cm-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The main characteristic-zero algebraicity assertion requires a complex CM abelian variety. Algebraicity after good-reduction specialization does not remove that hypothesis on the original complex variety. Blocked use: ['Apply the main Hodge theorem to arbitrary complex abelian varieties.', 'Use special-fiber algebraicity as a proof of characteristic-zero algebraicity.']
- **limit-rational-coefficients** (formal_scope): The cycle-class surjectivity assertion uses rational Chow groups and rational Betti cohomology. Integral surjectivity and control of torsion are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Replace the rational cycle-class map with an integral cycle-class map.', 'Promise integral representatives without clearing denominators.']
- **limit-all-scalar-conjugates** (hypothesis): Scalar descent requires algebraic coefficients and membership in the prescribed complex-linear subspace for every scalar conjugate. Membership for the original vector alone does not establish the stated descent conclusion. Blocked use: ['Descend a vector solely because its original complex realization has the desired Hodge type.', 'Apply the algebraic-coefficient descent lemma to an arbitrary transcendental-coefficient vector.']
- **limit-complete-type-line-transfer** (hypothesis): Embedding-line transfer requires agreement of the complete type functions, with the map extended by zero on the other source lines. Agreement at the distinguished embedding alone does not meet this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Transfer lines using only their original holomorphic or antiholomorphic type.', 'Discard separate copies of repeated rational summands during transfer.']
- **limit-balanced-switch-input** (hypothesis): The switch theorem assumes a Galois CM field and odd sign functions satisfying the stated balance relation. Conjugate Hodge balance identifies the relevant Hodge type; algebraicity requires the subsequent construction. Blocked use: ['Apply the switch to an unbalanced sign quadruple.', 'Treat conjugate Hodge balance as an independent algebraicity proof.']
- **limit-auxiliary-products-and-contractions** (formal_scope): The tensor reduction introduces auxiliary CM factors and composes transition tensors using nonzero polarization pairings. It does not establish low-codimension generation of the Hodge ring of a fixed power. Blocked use: ['Replace correspondence composition by cup products confined to the original fixed power.', 'Omit the intermediate polarization contractions or their nonvanishing checks.']
- **limit-projective-surface-and-rational-sources** (hypothesis): The surface criterion requires a smooth connected projective complex surface, rational Hodge maps from the prescribed CM modules, and a nonzero period of the selected embedding-line vectors. Complex one-form classes qualify through the stated rational-image spans. Blocked use: ['Apply the surface criterion to arbitrary complex one-forms without rational CM source maps.', 'Combine period factors that have not been placed on the same projective surface.', 'Infer algebraicity from a period whose nonvanishing has not been established.']
- **limit-surface-functional-versus-vector** (formal_scope): The pushed-forward surface class supplies a functional on the switch line. Algebraic divisor kernels and pushforward convert that functional into an algebraic tensor vector; the surface class itself is not that vector. Blocked use: ['Identify the pushed-forward surface class directly with the desired switch tensor.', 'Skip the divisor-kernel construction or division by the nonzero algebraic pairing.']
- **limit-cover-and-lift-conventions** (formal_scope): The unitary splitting is invoked in the circle-scalar extension. Its comparison with a double lift is made after restriction to the special-unitary group, and bare lifts outside the indicated linear subgroup retain their lift scalars. Blocked use: ['Assume a metaplectic double-cover splitting on every adelic unitary group.', 'Remove the scalar of a bare lift outside the stated special-linear subgroup.']
- **limit-finite-input-selection-before-level** (nonconstructive): Cotangent spanning is obtained using sparse-input limits. A common principal level is chosen after finitely many actual inputs have been selected, and no effective selection bound is supplied. Blocked use: ['Require the entire sparse-input sequence to descend to one common level.', 'Claim an effective spanning-input search bound from the limiting proof.']
- **limit-fixed-total-theta-variation** (hypothesis): The continuity comparison keeps the rational total space, total finite input, finite action, and compact quotient fixed while varying compact-place positive Lagrangians. Endpoint factorization and changes to individual input levels follow that comparison. Blocked use: ['Vary the total finite input or quotient while invoking the fixed-data continuity assertion.', 'Factor each varying parameter first and assume the resulting individual factors share the original level.']
- **limit-rational-endpoint-holomorphy** (approximation): Holomorphy of the projected top form is established at rational orthogonal decomposition endpoints. Intermediate compact-place parameters give smooth forms; their holomorphy is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Treat every intermediate parameter form as a holomorphic top form.', 'Infer holomorphy of the entire completed second Taylor tensor from the holomorphy of its double-alternating projection.']
- **limit-approximation-size-and-analytic-truncation** (approximation): Weak approximation and continuity provide a sufficiently close rational endpoint without an arithmetic-size bound. The analytic theta construction uses infinite convergent lattice sums; a certified finite truncation or explicit nonvanishing tolerance is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Infer denominator budgets for the approximating rational lines.', 'Certify period nonvanishing from an uncontrolled finite numerical theta sum.', 'Claim an explicit approximation radius from qualitative continuity.']
- **limit-full-moduli-components-and-markings** (hypothesis): The algebraic Hecke construction retains the full tuple space and all its components, including polarizations, field actions, and transported markings. Component preservation is separately proved for the active determinant-zero algebra. Blocked use: ['Restrict the full Hecke action to the original ball component without proving branch preservation.', 'Identify quotient targets using only their underlying unpolarized abelian varieties.', 'Discard transported markings when composing quotient branches.']
- **limit-good-split-prime-ordering** (hypothesis): The auxiliary prime is chosen after fixing the finite theta inputs and their common principal level, subject to good-reduction, complete-splitting, and finite-exclusion requirements. Ordinary density is established on every retained component. Blocked use: ['Choose an auxiliary prime before verifying the input and level exclusions.', 'Apply the ordinary-neighbor proof at a bad or nonsplit prime.', 'Assume ordinary density on the reference component alone suffices for the full Albanese identity.']
- **limit-raw-inverse-hecke-and-frobenius** (formal_scope): Geometric pullback on theta inputs is the inverse raw spherical action, with no degree averaging. The crystalline operator used in the polynomial is pullback of the special fiber's power Frobenius, rather than a reciprocal convention. Blocked use: ['Replace inverse spherical action by forward action in the theta parameter calculation.', 'Normalize branch sums by their degrees.', 'Substitute reciprocal Frobenius into the stated polynomial without changing conventions.']
- **limit-filtered-projector-purity** (hypothesis): Pure extreme Frobenius slope yields the asserted pure Hodge filtration only for an idempotent that preserves the filtration and commutes with Frobenius. The Hecke argument retains generalized character spaces and does not assume semisimplicity. Blocked use: ['Infer Hodge purity from an arbitrary slope projector.', 'Replace generalized Hecke spaces by diagonal eigenspaces without an independent semisimplicity argument.', 'Ignore nilpotent parts when claiming the full summand conclusion.']
- **limit-finite-family-common-cover** (hypothesis): The common-cover extraction conclusion applies to finite families of normalized sign functions and finite inputs. Persistence uses rational pullback to deeper principal levels; the auxiliary primes for different families need not agree. Blocked use: ['Assert a common extraction level for an unrestricted infinite input family.', 'Require all families to use the same auxiliary prime as a consequence of the corollary.', 'Combine extracted classes without pulling them to a common projective cover.']
- **limit-effective-generalized-hodge-support** (hypothesis): The generalized support conclusion requires an effective twist of the rational Hodge substructure on a complex CM abelian variety. The simple realization lemma assumes a nonzero simple polarizable CM Hodge structure; the support proof handles general substructures by their simple summands. Blocked use: ['Claim the stated support codimension without verifying effectivity.', 'Apply the simple realization lemma directly to an arbitrary nonsimple or nonpolarizable Hodge structure.']
- **limit-finite-field-tate-scope** (formal_scope): The Tate consequence concerns abelian varieties over finite fields with cohomological prime different from the characteristic. Its motivic implication does not assert an individual Hodge-class lift on a single CM lifting. Blocked use: ['Apply the Tate corollary to arbitrary projective finite-field varieties.', 'Use the displayed étale assertion at the characteristic prime.', 'Build a lifting procedure that assumes every Tate class comes from one CM lifting.']
- **limit-primitive-ample-positivity** (hypothesis): The positive intersection form is asserted on primitive rational numerical cycles for a rational ample divisor class in the stated codimension range, over an algebraically closed base field. Positivity on an arbitrary cycle space or for an arbitrary divisor is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Apply primitive positivity to the entire unprojected cycle space.', 'Replace the ample divisor class by an arbitrary divisor.', 'Remove the stated base-field or codimension restrictions.']
- **limit-no-cycle-extraction-runtime** (nonconstructive): The main theorem and its support consequences establish existence of algebraic representatives through analytic, arithmetic, and correspondence constructions. An executable cycle-equation extraction procedure, runtime bound, and finite certificate for the complete proof are ABSENT. Finite sign-switch calculations certify only their auxiliary combinatorial task. Blocked use: ['Advertise a cycle generator with a guaranteed runtime derived from surjectivity.', 'Treat a finite sign-switch or neighbor-count test as certification of the full CM Hodge theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the rational Hodge conjecture for complex abelian varieties with complex multiplication: every rational Hodge class on such a variety is a rational linear combination of algebraic cycle classes. As consequences, we obtain the generalized Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties, the Tate conjecture for abelian varieties over finite fields, and the Hodge standard conjecture for abelian varieties in arbitrary characteristic.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the rational Hodge conjecture asks whether every rational cohomology class of type $(p,p)$ is a rational linear combination of codimension-$p$ algebraic cycle classes. We prove this assertion for abelian varieties with complex multiplication, in every dimension and codimension.

A complex abelian variety $A$ is of *CM type*, or simply *CM*, if $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$ contains a commutative semisimple $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra of dimension $2\dim A$. We include the point by convention. Finite products and powers of CM abelian varieties are again CM.

<a id="stmt-thm-cm"></a>
<div id="thm:cm" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $A$ be a complex CM abelian variety. For every integer $p\geq0$, the Betti cycle-class map $$\operatorname{cl}_B:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^p(A)_{\mathbb{Q}}
