The original circle lift and this double lift, pushed out to the circle extension, differ by a continuous character of $G(F\otimes_\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{R})$. This group is connected and semisimple. The differential of such a character is zero, and connectedness makes the character trivial. The two lifts therefore agree. The rational compatibility of the global splitting now pairs $\omega_{P,f}(\gamma)$ with this infinite lift for every $\gamma\in G(F)$, proving the claimed rational covariance.

At a split finite place the two embedding summands are dual stable Lagrangians. If $h$ is the action on the position summand, the symplectic matrix is $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(h^{-t},h)$. The Schrödinger linear formula gives <a href="#eq:theta-linear-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-linear-action">[eq:theta-linear-action]</a>, up to the scalar of the chosen bare lift. On $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(F_v)$, the normalized substitution lift and the unitary lift have a character as their ratio. This character is trivial because $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(F_v)$ is perfect: elementary matrices generate it, and for distinct $i,j,k$, $$e_{ij}(u)=[e_{ik}(u),e_{kj}(1)].$$ The determinant factor is also $1$ there. This proves the split-place assertion without removing scalars from lifts outside $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$.

Finally the finite Weil action is smooth, so finitely many Schwartz functions have a common open stabilizer. Intersect it with the fixed lattice stabilizer in $G(\mathbb{A}_{F,f})$. Principal lattice congruence neighborhoods form a basis at the identity in that stabilizer, so a sufficiently deep principal one lies in the intersection. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

Restrict the tensors of Lemma <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a> to the period map from $D$. If a finite list of inputs is fixed by an open compact $K_f\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{F,f})$, the lemma just proved and <a href="#eq:theta-halfdet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-halfdet">[eq:theta-halfdet]</a> give their bundle covariance for $\Gamma=G(F)\cap K_f$. Passing to a torsion-free principal subgroup makes all of them descend to one compact quotient. This uses stabilizers in the smooth finite-adelic representation; it makes no claim that an arbitrary finite-index subgroup of a ball lattice is congruence.

<a id="subsec:theta-one-forms"></a>
#### Theta one-forms span cotangent fibers

For a line $W_i$, project $T_{1,\phi}$ to the $c\mu$ summand of the positive Lagrangian. Using our fixed complex trivialization of the constant line in <a href="#eq:theta-one-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-one-target">[eq:theta-one-target]</a>, denote the resulting one-form on $D$ by $\Theta_i(\phi)$.

<a id="stmt-prop-theta-one"></a>
<div id="prop:theta-one" class="proposition">

**Proposition 15**. *For every finite input $\phi$, the form $\Theta_i(\phi)$ is holomorphic on $D$ and descends to every sufficiently deep principal congruence quotient. At any fixed point of $D$, the values $\Theta_i(\phi)$, as $\phi$ varies over finite Schwartz inputs, span the cotangent fiber. Finitely many actual inputs already span that fiber and descend at a common sufficiently deep principal level.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-theta-one"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-theta-one</summary>

*Proof.* Holomorphy follows from that of $T_{1,\phi}$, the holomorphic embedding projection, and <a href="#eq:theta-cotangent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-cotangent">[eq:theta-cotangent]</a>. Descent follows from Lemma <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a>.

<a id="eq:theta-sparse-limit"></a>
Fix a point $x_*\in D$ and its period point $Z$. Here $a=3$. For $0\ne x_0\in\mathcal O_F^a$ and positive integers $k$, take $$\phi_k=\mathbf1_{\,x_0+k\widehat{\mathcal O}_F^{\,a}}.$$ Its rational support is exactly $x_0+k\mathcal O_F^a$. The first Taylor tensor is the ordinary gradient, so in the chosen graph frame $$T_{1,\phi_k}(Z)
  =2\pi i\sum_{x\in x_0+k\mathcal O_F^a}
      (x_\tau)_{\tau\in\Sigma}
       \exp\!\left(\pi i\sum_\tau x_\tau^tZ_\tau x_\tau\right).$$ Under the Minkowski embedding $\mathcal O_F^a$ is a full lattice. If $\rho>0$ is the minimum norm of a nonzero lattice vector, then, for $m\ne0$ in this lattice and $k\ge2\|x_0\|/\rho$, $$\|x_0+km\|\ge k\|m\|/2.$$ The Gaussian bound used above, with any polynomial factor absorbed into a weaker Gaussian, bounds the tail by a constant times a convergent lattice sum of the form $\sum_{m\ne0}\exp(-c k^2\|m\|^2)$. That sum tends to zero. The same argument is uniform for $Z$ in a compact neighborhood. We have proved $$\label{eq:theta-sparse-limit}
 T_{1,\phi_k}(Z)\longrightarrow
  2\pi i\,(x_{0,\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma}
       \exp\!\left(\pi i\sum_\tau
                       x_{0,\tau}^tZ_\tau x_{0,\tau}\right).$$

The scalar exponential is nonzero. As $x_0$ varies, the vectors $(x_{0,\tau})_\tau$ span the real position space, and their complex span is the entire first-tensor fiber in this frame. The complex span of the actual values $T_{1,\phi}(Z)$ is a subspace of a finite-dimensional space and hence is closed. It contains all the limits in <a href="#eq:theta-sparse-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-sparse-limit">[eq:theta-sparse-limit]</a>, so it is the full fiber. Projection onto $(\det\mathcal F_i)^{1/2}\otimes(\mathcal F_i)_{c\mu}$ is surjective; therefore the projected values span $\Omega_D^1|_{x_*}$. Choose finitely many actual inputs whose values contain a basis, and then apply the stabilizer assertion of Lemma <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a>.

The inputs $\phi_k$ in this limit need not have a common congruence stabilizer as $k$ varies. The limit takes place in one fiber over $D$; a common level is chosen only after finitely many actual inputs have been selected. ◻

</details>

On a compact Kähler quotient a holomorphic one-form is closed and harmonic. A nonzero such form therefore has nonzero cohomology class. These are complex classes. Nothing in this proposition identifies a rational CM source for them.

<a id="subsec:theta-rank-two"></a>
#### Rank-two tensors, wedges, and fixed-data variation

Take $W=W_1\perp W_2$, so $n=2$. Project $T_{2,\phi}$ to $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2((\mathcal F_W)_{c\mu})
=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2(Q^*\otimes W_\mu^*)$, and then apply $\operatorname{alt}_2$. Its target is the canonical bundle in <a href="#eq:theta-top-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-top-target">[eq:theta-top-target]</a>, up to a constant line. After trivializing that line, denote the resulting differential two-form by $\Xi_W(\phi)$. At a rational decomposition the product formula for theta series will express it as a finite sum of wedges of the one-forms just constructed. We then vary the compact-place data of the total space to compare two such expressions.

We now specify exactly what may vary in this construction. Keep fixed the rational Hermitian space $W$, the symplectic space $(\mathbb V_W,\psi_W)$, the rational embedding $G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb V_W)$, reference rational Schrödinger coordinates, the global Weil and unitary splitting choices, and the resulting finite action $\omega_{W,f}$. Also fix a single total finite input $\phi$ in these reference coordinates. At a compact place $\tau\ne\mu$, let $$W_\tau=W_\tau^+\perp W_\tau^-$$ range over orthogonal decompositions with $$\dim W_\tau^\pm=\#\{i\in\{1,2\}:d_i(\tau)=\pm1\}.$$ Require the form on $W_\tau^+$ to have sign $-\operatorname{sgn}(t_\tau)$, and the form on $W_\tau^-$ the opposite sign. Let $\mathcal T$ be the product of these parameter spaces. A definite local factor is a point; when the two signs differ, the local factor is the domain of lines of one prescribed sign in a Hermitian plane of signature $(1,1)$.

For $t\in\mathcal T$, the two compact-place pieces of the positive Lagrangian are $$V_\tau\otimes W_\tau^-,
 \qquad
 V_\tau^*\otimes(W_\tau^+)^*.$$ In the second expression $(W_\tau^+)^*$ is embedded as $\operatorname{ann}(W_\tau^-)\subset W_\tau^*$; the orthogonal decomposition identifies it with the dual of $W_\tau^+$. The active pieces remain, exactly, $$L\otimes W_\mu,\qquad Q^*\otimes W_\mu^*.$$ They depend only on $W_\mu$, not on any decomposition of it. These prescriptions give positive Lagrangians for all $t\in\mathcal T$. The same projection as in <a href="#eq:theta-top-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-top-target">[eq:theta-top-target]</a>, with a local smooth choice of constant-line trivialization, defines a smooth form of type $(2,0)$, denoted $\Xi_W(\phi;t)$, even when $t$ is not induced by rational lines. We do not claim it is holomorphic for such an intermediate parameter.

<a id="stmt-prop-theta-top"></a>
<div id="prop:theta-top" class="proposition">

**Proposition 16**. *The rank-two construction has the following properties.*

1.  *At period data induced by a rational orthogonal decomposition $W=W_1\perp W_2$, the form $\Xi_W(\phi)$ is holomorphic. On a sufficiently deep common principal quotient it is a finite sum, with constant coefficients, of forms $\Theta_1(\phi_{1,r})\wedge\Theta_2(\phi_{2,r})$.*

2.  *There is an input which is a product in adapted rational coordinates and for which $\Xi_W(\phi)$ is nonzero.*

3.  *Fix $W$, the total finite input $\phi$, and a torsion-free principal quotient $X$ whose finite level fixes $\phi$ under $\omega_{W,f}$. With locally smooth constant-line trivializations, the map $$t\longmapsto\Xi_W(\phi;t)$$ is continuous in the $C^\infty$ topology of differential forms on this fixed compact quotient. The same finite level works throughout the parameter neighborhood.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-theta-top"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-theta-top</summary>

*Proof.* For (i), the rational orthogonal decomposition gives a rational symplectic direct sum $\mathbb V_W=\mathbb V_{W_1}\perp\mathbb V_{W_2}$. Choose rational coordinates adapted to it. By Remark <a href="#rem:theta-adapted-coordinates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:theta-adapted-coordinates">13</a>, the total reference input becomes another finite Schwartz input in the adapted coordinates. Such an input is a finite sum $$\sum_r\phi_{1,r}\otimes\phi_{2,r}.$$ Indeed, a locally constant compactly supported function on the product of two finite-adelic vector spaces is invariant under a sufficiently small product compact open subgroup. Only finitely many of its cosets meet the support, and each coset is a product coset.

For a product input the completed theta series multiply, since both the sum and its completion split in the adapted coordinates. If $T_j^{(i)}$ denotes the degree-$j$ coefficient for the $i$-th line, the coefficient of total degree two is <a id="eq:theta-quadratic-product"></a>$$\label{eq:theta-quadratic-product}
 T_2^{(1)}T_0^{(2)}
       +T_1^{(1)}T_1^{(2)}
       +T_0^{(1)}T_2^{(2)}.$$ The coefficient of the mixed product is $1$, by the $1/k!$ normalization in <a href="#eq:theta-completion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-completion">[eq:theta-completion]</a>. The two unmixed terms have both multiplicity factors in the same one-dimensional $(W_i)_\mu^*$; they vanish under $\operatorname{alt}_2$. The mixed term becomes the wedge of the two projected first tensors. With compatible constant-line trivializations it is exactly $\Theta_1(\phi_{1,r})\wedge\Theta_2(\phi_{2,r})$; other trivializations multiply it by a fixed nonzero scalar. Rational changes of coordinates are already accounted for by <a href="#eq:theta-taylor-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-taylor-law">[eq:theta-taylor-law]</a>. Moreover, two identifications with the differential bundle used here have a ratio invariant under the transitive infinite special-unitary action on $D$, so that ratio is constant. This proves the wedge expansion and its holomorphy. We have proved holomorphy only after the double-alternating projection: the whole completed second Taylor tensor need not be holomorphic. The finitely many individual inputs descend on a further common principal level by Lemma <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a>.

For (ii), fix a point of $D$. Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-one" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-one">15</a> provides an input for $W_1$ with a nonzero cotangent value and an input for $W_2$ whose value is outside the line it spans. Their wedge is nonzero at that point. The product of these inputs in adapted coordinates has a nonzero double-alternating second tensor by the calculation above. Transporting this product back to the reference coordinates gives a total finite input with $\Xi_W(\phi)\ne0$.

For (iii), no rational coordinate change is made as $t$ varies. The rational symplectic space, its rational $G$-action, the finite representation, and the total input all remain fixed. The changed compact-place Lagrangians remain invariant under $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(3)$, since all changes are in the multiplicity factor. Their determinant characters on $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(3)$ are trivial. The active pieces have not changed, so their determinant character remains $\ell^4$. The infinite comparison in Lemma <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a> therefore gives the same half-determinant action $L^2$ at every $t$. Consequently the same subgroup fixing $\phi$ gives equivariance and descent at every parameter. There is no finite translation, Hecke choice, or change of level in this variation.

It remains to check uniformity on the quotient. The constant determinant factors in <a href="#eq:theta-halfdet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-halfdet">[eq:theta-halfdet]</a> vary smoothly with the compact-place subspaces. On a small parameter neighborhood choose smooth nonzero frames and their square roots, agreeing with the chosen frames at the center. Cover the fixed compact $X$ by finitely many charts with precompact lifts to $D$. On the closure of each lifted chart times a sufficiently small compact parameter neighborhood, the period map is smooth, its image is a compact subset of the Siegel domain, and every fixed order of its derivatives is bounded. The imaginary parts thus have a common positive lower bound. The rational support of the fixed $\phi$ continues to lie in one fixed finite union of lattice cosets. The Gaussian estimates following <a href="#eq:theta-completion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-completion">[eq:theta-completion]</a> now give uniform convergence of every fixed order of derivatives in the chart coordinates and the parameters. Projection to <a href="#eq:theta-top-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-top-target">[eq:theta-top-target]</a> and the chosen smooth trivializations preserve these bounds. The unchanged equivariance glues these convergent local expressions on $X$. This is continuity in the $C^\infty$ topology. Compactness of $X$ is used here; no estimate at a cusp or boundary is needed. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:theta-approximation"></a>
#### Approximation while the total theta input stays fixed

We now apply the last proposition to the sign switch. The approximation occurs in $\mathcal T$, before the rational coordinate changes used to factor either endpoint.

<a id="stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity"></a>
<div id="cor:theta-switch-continuity" class="corollary">

**Corollary 17**. *Suppose $d_1+d_2=d_3+d_4$, with $d_i(\mu)=1$ for all $i$. Start with $W=W_1\perp W_2$ as above, let $t_0$ be its compact-place data, and choose a total input $\phi$ for which $\xi=\Xi_W(\phi;t_0)$ is a nonzero holomorphic top form on a sufficiently deep compact principal quotient $X$. There are rational orthogonal decompositions $W=W_3\perp W_4$ with line signs $d_3,d_4$ whose total compact-place positive Lagrangians are arbitrarily close to those at $t_0$. The total active positive Lagrangian is exactly unchanged.*

<a id="eq:theta-nonzero-pairing"></a>
*For a sufficiently close rational choice $t'$, the form $\xi'=\Xi_W(\phi;t')$ is holomorphic on the same quotient $X$, with the same total finite input, and $$\label{eq:theta-nonzero-pairing}
 \int_X\xi\wedge\overline{\xi'}\ne0.$$ Both endpoints admit the wedge expansions of Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-top">16</a> on a further common principal cover, and their pairing remains nonzero there.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-theta-switch-continuity</summary>

*Proof.* At a compact place $\tau\ne\mu$, equality of the two sign sums says exactly that the multisets $\{d_1(\tau),d_2(\tau)\}$ and $\{d_3(\tau),d_4(\tau)\}$ coincide. Reorder the two original local lines so that the first has the sign required by $d_3(\tau)$, and choose a nonzero vector on it. After fixing an $E$-basis of $W$, weak approximation in $E$, coordinate by coordinate at these finitely many complex places, gives a single $w\in W(E)$ arbitrarily close to all the chosen local vectors. Nondegeneracy and the signs of their Hermitian norms are open conditions. For a sufficiently close approximation, $$W_3=Ew,\qquad W_4=W_3^\perp$$ is therefore a rational orthogonal decomposition. Its first line has the prescribed compact-place signs for $d_3$; orthogonal inertia gives the complementary signs for $d_4$. At $\mu$ the form on $W_\mu$ is definite, so both new lines automatically have the same required sign there.

The total compact-place Lagrangians converge to the original ones. Reordering the original lines only relabels their total positive summands. For a nondegenerate local vector the orthogonal projection onto its line is $$z\longmapsto h_W(z,w)h_W(w,w)^{-1}w.$$ It varies continuously with $w$, so both the line and its orthogonal complement converge to the chosen original ones. The selected signed subspaces, and hence the total positive Lagrangians, converge. At the active pair the total pieces are always $L\otimes W_\mu$ and $Q^*\otimes W_\mu^*$, so they are unchanged, not merely close. The approximating rational lines may have small active norms or large arithmetic denominators. No bound on these is needed: the active total period data are fixed, and the continuity assertion concerns the total tensor in the original reference coordinates.

All the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-top">16</a>(iii) now hold with the same rational $W$, the same $G$, the same total finite $\phi$, and the same compact $X$. Thus $$\Xi_W(\phi;t')\longrightarrow \Xi_W(\phi;t_0)$$ smoothly on $X$. The limit $\xi$ is a nonzero holomorphic top form. In local complex coordinates $\xi=f\,dz_1\wedge dz_2$, the form $\xi\wedge\bar\xi$ is a positive multiple of $|f|^2\,dx_1\wedge dy_1\wedge dx_2\wedge dy_2$. Consequently $\int_X\xi\wedge\bar\xi>0$. Continuity of the integral gives <a href="#eq:theta-nonzero-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-nonzero-pairing">[eq:theta-nonzero-pairing]</a> for all sufficiently close rational choices. At each such endpoint holomorphy follows from Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-top">16</a>(i), not from a holomorphy assertion along the intermediate parameter path.

<a id="eq:theta-cover-pairing"></a>
Factor the two endpoints only now. There are finitely many line inputs in their two wedge expansions. Choose a principal subgroup contained both in the level of $X$ and in their finite stabilizers. It gives a finite unramified cover $\pi:X'\to X$ on which all individual factors descend. The covering formula is $$\label{eq:theta-cover-pairing}
 \int_{X'}\pi^*\xi\wedge\overline{\pi^*\xi'}
       =\deg(\pi)\int_X\xi\wedge\overline{\xi'}\ne0.$$ Both quotients are compact and torsion-free, so there is no cusp term or ramification correction. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

Expansion of the two pulled-back endpoint forms on $X'$ in <a href="#eq:theta-cover-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-cover-pairing">[eq:theta-cover-pairing]</a> gives <a href="#eq:theta-mixed-period-goal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-mixed-period-goal">[eq:theta-mixed-period-goal]</a>; in particular at least one pair of individual wedge terms has nonzero mixed period. This is the analytic nonvanishing needed for Proposition <a href="#prop:surface-switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:surface-switch">9</a>. To apply that criterion we must still express the relevant one-form classes as complex linear combinations of images of the selected CM lines under rational Hodge maps, on a common projective principal cover. That arithmetic step is separate from the approximation just proved.

<a id="sec:arithmetic"></a>
### Projective ball quotients and a Frobenius identity

The theta construction has supplied complex one-form classes on compact ball quotients. To use the surface criterion, we must identify their rational sources: for the sign function $v$, the relevant class must lie in the complex span of the images of $U(v)_1$ under rational Hodge maps from $U(v)$. This section and Section <a href="#sec:cm-sources" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cm-sources">6</a> prove the following extraction theorem. We first recall its input data from the theta construction.

Retain the notation of Section <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a>. Thus $E\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ is Galois CM, $F=E^+$, $G_E=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(E/\mathbb{Q})$, and $c\in G_E$ is complex conjugation. We assume $s=[F:\mathbb{Q}]\geq2$. The Hermitian $E/F$-space $V$ has dimension three, signature $(2,1)$ at $\mu=1$, and is positive definite at the other real places. Write $G=\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(V)$, let $D$ be its ball of negative lines, and retain the fixed $\mathcal O_E$-lattice $\Lambda\subset V$. Fix an odd function $v:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ with $v(1)=1$, and put $$d(\tau)=v(\tau^{-1}).$$ For one multiplicity line $P$ with these compact-place signs, write $\Theta(\phi)$ for its theta one-form and abbreviate its finite special-unitary action to $\omega_f=\omega_{P,f}$. We work with a finite collection of inputs at a common principal level. The auxiliary prime will be chosen only after that level is fixed.

<a id="stmt-thm-cm-extraction"></a>
<div id="thm:cm-extraction" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:cm-extraction-span"></a>
**Theorem 18** (Degree-one CM extraction). *Let $v:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd with $v(1)=1$, and use the multiplicity-line theta construction prescribed by $d(\tau)=v(\tau^{-1})$. Given a finite collection of its finite adelic Schwartz inputs, there is a common sufficiently deep principal ball quotient $S_0=\Gamma\backslash D$ on which every associated theta one-form class belongs to $$\label{eq:cm-extraction-span}
\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{
f\bigl(U(v)_1\bigr):
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\bigl(U(v),H^1(S_0,\mathbb{Q})\bigr)
\right\}.$$ The maps in this formula are rational Hodge maps, and their images are viewed after extension to $\mathbb{C}$. The conclusion persists after passage to a deeper principal level.*

</div>

The quotient $S_0$ will be a smooth connected projective surface. Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-family-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-family-extraction">29</a> gives the version needed for the surface criterion: finitely many families with different sign functions satisfy these span conclusions on one projective principal cover of any prescribed torsion-free principal base quotient. The reference moduli construction depends only on $V$, so the same geometry serves every multiplicity line.

We begin by realizing the principal quotients as components of a full projective moduli space of polarized abelian varieties with $E$-action. Keeping all components makes the isogeny correspondences into algebraic Hecke operators on the product of their Albanese varieties. Dense ordinary loci then let us prove an integral Frobenius polynomial for these operators. Section <a href="#sec:cm-sources" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cm-sources">6</a> combines that polynomial with the active theta parameter to determine the Hodge types of the generalized Hecke spaces containing the theta classes. Scalar descent then proves the extraction theorem.

<a id="subsec:reference-moduli"></a>
#### A projective moduli space for the reference three-space

The reference abelian varieties use $V$, not $V\otimes_E P$. For a negative line $L\in D$, prescribe on the complexified homology $$\mathcal F_\mu=L,\qquad
\mathcal F_c=\operatorname{ann}(L)\subset V_\mu^*,$$ where the Hermitian form identifies the conjugate summand with the complex-linear dual. At each other conjugate pair choose, once and for all, the whole summand on one side and zero on the other. These annihilator prescriptions together form a Lagrangian. An imaginary element $\delta_{\mathrm{ref}}\in E^\times$, whose signs are chosen by weak approximation in $F$, makes them positive for $$\psi_{\mathrm{ref}}(x,y)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\delta_{\mathrm{ref}}h_V(x,y)\bigr).$$ Multiplying $\delta_{\mathrm{ref}}$ by a positive integer makes the form integral on $\Lambda$.

<a id="eq:reference-lie-ranks"></a>
Here the homology filtration is the kernel of the Lie quotient. If $J$ is the complex structure on real homology, then $z=u+iJu$ lies in that filtration and $$\psi_{\mathrm{ref}}(z,\overline z)/i
   =-2\psi_{\mathrm{ref}}(u,Ju).$$ Thus $-\psi_{\mathrm{ref}}$ is the Riemann form in the positivity convention of Section <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a>. The corresponding abelian varieties have dimension $3s$, and their Lie ranks are $$\label{eq:reference-lie-ranks}
r_\mu=2,\qquad r_c=1,\qquad
\{r_\tau,r_{c\tau}\}=\{0,3\}
\quad\bigl(\tau\notin\{1,c\}\bigr).$$ Fix their polarization type.

Choose $N$ sufficiently divisible to give a fine abelian moduli problem, a torsion-free principal lattice level, and a level fixing the chosen finite theta inputs. We will also require the level to kill all possible unitary determinant roots of unity. A marking means a full ordered basis of $N$-torsion; we allow every pairing matrix for that basis. In particular, transporting it through an isogeny of degree prime to $N$ is allowed even when the isogeny rescales the polarization pairing.

We retain all components so that the full isogeny correspondences can be defined without assuming that the reference component is preserved. Over a number field $K_0\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ containing $E$, consider the reduced scheme $S$ of tuples $$(B,\lambda,\iota_E,\eta_N).$$ Here $B$ is an abelian variety of dimension $3s$, $\lambda$ is a polarization of the fixed type, $\iota_E:\mathcal O_E\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(B)$ is a unital action whose Rosati adjoint is $c$, $\eta_N$ is the full marking, and each Lie summand has the particular rank fixed above (with values displayed in <a href="#eq:reference-lie-ranks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:reference-lie-ranks">[eq:reference-lie-ranks]</a>). We retain all connected components of this tuple space.

<a id="stmt-lem-reference-moduli"></a>
<div id="lem:reference-moduli" class="lemma">

**Lemma 19**. *The scheme $S$ is of finite type, smooth, and projective over $K_0$. Its complex connected components are compact ball quotients. For sufficiently divisible $N$, the component arising from $\Lambda$ is the quotient of $D$ by its principal special-unitary level.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-reference-moduli"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-reference-moduli</summary>

*Proof.* The Lie-rank condition need not identify a single rational Hermitian isomorphism class. We therefore construct the whole finite-type action locus, identify its complex components as ball quotients, and then prove projectivity and identify the component of $\Lambda$.

*The finite-type action locus.* The fine-moduli theorem for polarized abelian varieties gives a quasi-projective scheme with universal polarization and full level at sufficiently large invertible level (Mumford and Fogarty 1982, vol. 34, chap. 7, Definitions 7.1–7.2, Proposition 7.6, and Theorem 7.9) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[MumfordFogarty1982](#ref-MumfordFogarty1982)]. In that theorem the polarization degree is fixed and the level is an ordered torsion basis, with no prescribed pairing matrix. After inverting the polarization degree, the polarization kernel is finite étale, so its geometric elementary-divisor type is locally constant. We may therefore take the open-and-closed part of the required type. If necessary, first apply the theorem to a sufficiently large divisor of $N$ prime to the polarization degree and then add the remaining full $N$-basis as a finite étale level cover. These constructions work over a ring of integers with finitely many primes inverted, as well as in characteristic zero.

We next impose the action by a finite-type construction. Choose finitely many ring generators $b$ of $\mathcal O_E$ and parameterize their graphs in relative Hilbert schemes of the square of the universal abelian scheme. The degrees of the graphs in question are bounded. Indeed, put $\alpha=1+\bar b b$. It is a nonzero totally positive element of $F$. Choose a positive integer $m$ with $m\alpha^{-1}\in\mathcal O_E$. In every such action $\alpha$ is an isogeny, and $$\deg(\alpha)\deg(m\alpha^{-1})=m^{6s}.$$ The second factor is a positive integer, so $\deg(\alpha)$ is bounded independently of the point.

To translate this into a Hilbert bound, the normalized Poincaré bundle on the universal abelian scheme times its dual, pulled back by $(\mathop{\mathrm{id}},\lambda)$, is a relatively ample bundle inducing $2\lambda$. The tensor product of its two pullbacks to the square restricts on the graph of $b$ to a bundle inducing $2\lambda(1+\bar b b)$. Its polarization degree is bounded by the preceding identity. Riemann–Roch on an abelian variety of dimension $3s$ gives the Hilbert polynomial $\chi(\mathcal L)n^{3s}$, with $\chi(\mathcal L)^2=\deg(\phi_{\mathcal L})$. Only finitely many polynomials can therefore occur. On their graph loci impose the homomorphism, ring, Rosati, and Lie-rank identities. This gives a finite-type action locus.

Over the characteristic-zero polarized base this locus is unramified. One form of rigidity suffices: an infinitesimal difference of two homomorphisms between fixed abelian schemes would give a homomorphism from a proper connected abelian fiber to an additive tangent group, and must vanish. The locus is also proper over that base. Given a discrete valuation ring mapping to the polarized base and a lift of its generic point to the action locus, each generator homomorphism extends uniquely between the fixed abelian schemes by the Néron mapping property (Bosch, Lütkebohmert, and Raynaud 1990, vol. 21, sec. 1.2, Definition 1 and Proposition 8) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990](#ref-BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990)]. Its graph remains a graph of the same Hilbert polynomial. The ring and Rosati identities extend by uniqueness. Since the base contains $E$, the action algebra splits into idempotents in characteristic zero, and their Lie summands have locally constant ranks. Thus the extended point lies in the same action locus. The valuative criterion gives properness. A proper finite-type unramified morphism is quasi-finite and hence finite, so the action locus is finite over the polarized base.

*The complex components.* We now identify the reduced complex structure of this algebraic locus. In a marked period chart, the integral matrices of the action are locally constant. Such a matrix acts on the complex torus exactly when it preserves the homology filtration; the resulting holomorphic endomorphism of a polarized complex torus is algebraic. Hence the reduced local locus is precisely the locus of positive filtrations stable under the fixed matrices. Trace duality and the Rosati identity express the alternating form as an imaginary trace multiple of an $E/F$-Hermitian form on the rational homology, of $E$-dimension three. Isotropy makes the two filtrations in a conjugate pair annihilators. Positivity and <a href="#eq:reference-lie-ranks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:reference-lie-ranks">[eq:reference-lie-ranks]</a> leave one negative line at the active place and constant data at every other place. The local locus is therefore a smooth complex two-ball.

This description gives whole ball quotients, not only local ball charts. Fix a marked polarized $E$-lattice occurring in $S$. Every positive filtration in its compatible ball gives a complex torus with the fixed integral Riemann form, and hence an abelian variety; the preserving integral matrices give its algebraic $\mathcal O_E$-action. Conversely every tuple with these lattice data gives such a filtration. Its connected component is thus the quotient of the whole ball by the marked lattice stabilizer.

*Projectivity and the principal level.* For each such rational Hermitian space, a vector isotropic over $E$ would remain isotropic at a definite real place. Since $s\geq2$, there is no such vector, and its special-unitary group is $F$-anisotropic. The arithmetic lattice and compactness theorems give compact ball quotients (Borel and Harish-Chandra 1962) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[BorelHarishChandra1962](#ref-BorelHarishChandra1962)]; see (Milne 2005, Theorem 3.3 and Proposition 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[MilneShimura2017](#ref-MilneShimura2017)]. The level removes stabilizers. There are finitely many components because $S$ is of finite type. The finite action locus is quasi-projective, and its reduced complex space is smooth with compact components. It is therefore proper, and a proper quasi-projective scheme is projective. Smoothness and properness descend from the complex base change to $K_0$.

Finally the initial lattice $\Lambda$ is among these data. Even if it is not free, its determinant module is a fractional $\mathcal O_E$-ideal. The determinant of a unitary lattice automorphism is consequently a unit of $\mathcal O_E$ of norm one. All its complex conjugates have modulus one, so it is a root of unity. A sufficiently divisible principal level kills every nontrivial such determinant. Its stabilizer is then precisely the principal special-unitary lattice level, as claimed. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:good-ordinary-models"></a>
#### Good models and ordinary points on every component

Enlarge $K_0$ to a number field $K\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ over which all connected components $S_j$ are geometrically connected and have $K$-points. Index the original ball component by $S_0$. Use those points as origins for the Albanese maps $$a_j:S_j\longrightarrow A_j=\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(S_j),\qquad A_S=\prod_j A_j.$$ We regard each $A_j$ as a factor of $A_S$, with zero in the other factors. The Albanese pullbacks give an isomorphism of rational Hodge structures <a id="eq:albanese-h1"></a>$$\label{eq:albanese-h1}
H^1(A_S,\mathbb{Q})\simeq H^1(S,\mathbb{Q}).$$

Because the Frobenius identity will be checked on ordinary points and then on Albanese differences, we need density on every component and generation of each reduced Albanese factor by those differences.

<a id="stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes"></a>
<div id="lem:good-ordinary-primes" class="lemma">

**Lemma 20**. *After one finite extension of $K$ and removal of finitely many primes, the $S_j$ have smooth proper models with geometrically connected fibers, the $A_j$ extend to abelian schemes, and the $a_j$ extend to the models. The model of $S$ lies in the full tuple parameter scheme, retaining the $\mathcal O_E$-action and its graph coordinates. Differences of Albanese images generate every geometric fiber of each $A_j$.*

*At every remaining rational prime $q$ completely split in $K$, the ordinary locus of the universal abelian scheme is dense in each geometric component of the special fiber of $S$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-good-ordinary-primes</summary>

*Proof.* *Models and Albanese generation.* Spread the finitely many schemes, morphisms, and universal data over an open part of the ring of integers of $K$. One can obtain the proper model of $S$ by taking its closure in a projective compactification of the full parameter scheme used in the proof of Lemma <a href="#lem:reference-moduli" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:reference-moduli">19</a>. Its intersection with the boundary has proper closed image on the integer base. That image misses the generic point because $S$ is proper and lies in the parameter scheme. Removing the finitely many primes in the image leaves the closure inside the full parameter scheme. Thus its geometric points are still distinguished by their polarized, acted-on, marked tuples, including the graph coordinates. Further shrinking gives smooth proper models with geometrically connected component fibers, good abelian models, and the extended Albanese maps.

For a component $S_j$, let $\Delta_j=a_j(S_j)-a_j(S_j)\subset A_j$. It is closed, irreducible, symmetric, and contains zero. The successive sum images $n\Delta_j$ are nested irreducible closed sets. Their dimensions eventually stabilize; then equality of two successive sets implies stability under addition by $\Delta_j$. Symmetry shows that the stable image is the abelian subvariety generated by $\Delta_j$, which is $A_j$ by the Albanese property. Thus for some fixed $n$ the proper sum map $$(S_j\times S_j)^n\longrightarrow A_j$$ is surjective. Spread this map together with the other data. Its closed image contains the generic fiber of the flat abelian scheme; that fiber is dense, so the image is the whole abelian scheme. Consequently the sum map is surjective on every geometric fiber.

*Ordinary points.* For each characteristic-zero component, we now choose a point whose reduction at the split primes retained below is ordinary. In its rational Hermitian three-space choose, by weak approximation, an $E$-line negative at $\mu$, and diagonalize its orthogonal complement. At the period whose negative line is this coordinate line, the three rational orthogonal projectors split the rational homology into three $E$-lines and preserve the filtration. Integral multiples of them are endomorphisms of the corresponding abelian variety.

These points and endomorphisms are defined over $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$. Indeed, add their graphs as extra parameters with the Hilbert polynomials at the selected point. This is a finite-type parameter scheme over a number field. Locally the projector matrices are fixed by rigidity. A negative line preserved by all three projectors must be the unique negative coordinate line, so the selected enhanced point is isolated. An isolated complex point of a finite-type scheme over a number field has algebraic coordinates. Adjoin the finitely many fields of definition to $K$, spread these points and endomorphisms, and exclude their projector and isogeny denominators.

Now let $q$ be a remaining completely split prime. At each selected point the integral projectors and the idempotents of $\mathcal O_E\otimes\mathbb{Z}_q$ split the $q$-divisible group into pieces of height one. Their heights are the corresponding characteristic-zero Tate ranks and stay constant under specialization. A height-one $q$-divisible group over an algebraically closed field has dimension zero or one. In the first case it is étale; in the second its dual has dimension zero, so it is multiplicative. Thus each selected reduction is ordinary. Ordinariness is open, and a smooth geometrically connected fiber is geometrically irreducible. This nonempty open is dense on each component. Chebotarev’s theorem supplies infinitely many completely split rational primes after the finite exclusions. ◻

</details>

Choose such a rational prime $q$, also prime to the polarization degree, $N$, the discriminant of $E$, and all exceptional local data of the finite theta inputs. Fix an embedding $$\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_q$$ and normalize $\nu_\iota$ by $\nu_\iota(q)=1$. The resulting completion of $K$ is $\mathbb{Q}_q$, with residue field $\mathbb{F}_q$. For $\tau\in G_E$, let $\mathfrak p_\tau$ be the prime of $E$ defined by $\iota\circ\tau$. Thus for $x\in E^\times$, $$\nu_\iota(\tau(x))=\operatorname{val}_{\mathfrak p_\tau}(x).$$ Select one prime from each conjugate pair, including <a id="eq:active-prime"></a>$$\label{eq:active-prime}
w=\mathfrak p_c,\qquad \bar w=\mathfrak p_1.$$ Write $\mathcal J$ for this set of selected blocks. The $w$-block has height three and Lie rank one; every other selected block has height three and Lie rank zero or three. The opposite blocks are their polarization duals. These rank assertions persist on the model because the corresponding idempotent Lie and torsion summands are locally free. Choosing $w=\mathfrak p_c$, rather than $\mathfrak p_1$, will fix the conjugate-valuation signs.

<a id="subsec:algebraic-hecke"></a>
#### Algebraic Hecke operators on the Albanese

Choose bases for the selected $q$-adic lattices and the opposite dual lattices. Since the polarization is prime to $q$, the split local similitude group and its integral subgroup are $$\mathscr G_q=
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal J}\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q)\right)\times\mathbb{Q}_q^\times,
\qquad
\mathscr K_q=
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal J}\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)\right)\times\mathbb{Z}_q^\times.$$ The last coordinate is the common polarization multiplier. Use the raw spherical Hecke algebra with $\operatorname{vol}(\mathscr K_q)=1$. Its integral basis consists of double-coset characteristic functions; its rational extension will act on the Albanese up to isogeny. A double coset is specified by a multiplier exponent $h\in\mathbb{Z}$ and, on each selected block, an unordered triple of elementary exponents $(a_{j,1},a_{j,2},a_{j,3})$. For a representative $g_j$ with those exponents, our target position is the overlattice $g_j^{-1}\Lambda_j$; branches are represented modulo the integral subgroup on the left.

<a id="eq:positive-hecke-range"></a>
<a id="eq:hecke-polarization"></a>
First suppose that $$\label{eq:positive-hecke-range}
h\geq0,\qquad 0\leq a_{j,k}\leq h.$$ On the $j$-block of $B[q^h]$, choose a subgroup of the indicated elementary divisors. On the opposite block choose its annihilator under the perfect $q^h$-torsion pairing. The total subgroup $H$ is maximal isotropic: each conjugate pair contributes order $q^{3h}$, so its total order is $q^{3sh}$. Its quotient $f:B\to B'=B/H$ is required to have polarization $$\label{eq:hecke-polarization}
f^\vee\lambda'f=q^h\lambda.$$ We verify that this is an algebraic construction in families, including the polarization homomorphism.

Over the characteristic-zero parameter scheme the torsion and the subgroups are finite étale. The translation quotient exists as an fppf scheme quotient for the free finite-locally-free action (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tags 07S6 and 07S7) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject)]. Its group law descends, and the finite étale quotient of the abelian scheme is again an abelian scheme.

On a complex fiber the polarization in <a href="#eq:hecke-polarization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-polarization">[eq:hecke-polarization]</a> is seen directly on lattices. If $\Lambda_j\subset\Lambda_j'\subset q^{-h}\Lambda_j$ is the selected target overlattice and the multiplier is $q^h$, its opposite lattice is $$q^{-h}(\Lambda_j')^\vee.$$ The $q^h$-scaled alternating form is integral and perfect on this pair at $q$, by the annihilator condition. At every other prime the $q$-isogeny changes no elementary-divisor valuation. The scaled form is positive, so it gives a Riemann polarization on the quotient of the original type. More generally, the field-level isotropic descent criterion gives the unique symmetric polarization homomorphism on every geometric fiber (Edixhoven, Geer, and Moonen 2011, chap. XI, Proposition 11.25) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[EdixhovenVanDerGeerMoonen2011Polarizations](#ref-EdixhovenVanDerGeerMoonen2011Polarizations)].

Here is the relative descent from this fiber calculation. Since $H\subset B[q^h]$, multiplication by $q^h$ descends to $g:B'\to B$, with $$gf=[q^h]_B,\qquad fg=[q^h]_{B'}.$$ Put $\beta=g^\vee\lambda g:B'\to B'^\vee$. On each geometric fiber the preceding polarization satisfies $\beta=q^h\lambda'$, so $\beta$ kills $B'[q^h]$. The correspondence parameter scheme is smooth and reduced, and $B'[q^h]$ is finite étale over it, hence reduced. Vanishing on its geometric points is therefore vanishing of the relative morphism. The fppf quotient by $B'[q^h]$ now factors $\beta$ uniquely through $[q^h]_{B'}$. It gives a relative homomorphism $\lambda'$ with $\beta=q^h\lambda'$. Its fibers are the symmetric polarizations just constructed, and cancellation of isogenies gives <a href="#eq:hecke-polarization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-polarization">[eq:hecke-polarization]</a>. This also proves uniqueness. We have descended the polarization homomorphism, not a particular line bundle.

The $\mathcal O_E$-action descends because $H$ is stable. Cancelling $f$ in <a href="#eq:hecke-polarization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-polarization">[eq:hecke-polarization]</a> shows that its Rosati adjoint remains $c$. The characteristic-zero Lie ranks are unchanged, and $f$ transports the full $N$-basis. Thus the quotient is a point of the same full tuple space. The subgroup choices form a finite étale scheme over $S$; the quotient construction is its algebraic target map. Distinct kernels remain distinct branches even when their target tuples are isomorphic.

<a id="stmt-lem-hecke-albanese"></a>
<div id="lem:hecke-albanese" class="lemma">

**Lemma 21**. *The rational spherical Hecke algebra of $(\mathscr G_q,\mathscr K_q)$ acts in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(A_S)\otimes\mathbb{Q}$ and, by pullback, on $H^1(S,\mathbb{Q})$. A basis operator in the positive range <a href="#eq:positive-hecke-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-hecke-range">[eq:positive-hecke-range]</a> is an integral homomorphism defined over $K$. Its pullback on one-forms is the sum of pullbacks over its target branches.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-hecke-albanese"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-hecke-albanese</summary>

<a id="eq:hecke-albanese-differences"></a>
*Proof.* Let $C\to S$ be one of the finite étale source correspondences. On a source component, compose each target branch with the appropriate Albanese map and sum in $A_S$. This sum descends from a cover trivializing the branches, because it is invariant under their permutations. It is a morphism $n_j:S_j\to A_S$. Subtracting $n_j$ at the basepoint and applying the Albanese universal property gives an integral homomorphism $A_j\to A_S$. On differences it has the exact formula $$\label{eq:hecke-albanese-differences}
T\bigl(a_j(x)-a_j(y)\bigr)
 =\sum_{z\in C_x}a_{j(z)}(t(z))
  -\sum_{z\in C_y}a_{j(z)}(t(z)),$$ where $t$ is the target map, $j(z)$ indexes the component containing $t(z)$, fibers are counted with their branches, and $a_{j(z)}$ is included in $A_S$. These homomorphisms on the factors define the operator on $A_S$. Their pullback on one-forms is the branch sum, since a constant translation contributes no differential.

Composition has the raw spherical convolution multiplicities. For a selected lattice $\Lambda_j$, a branch is an overlattice $g_j^{-1}\Lambda_j$ with a representative modulo the integral subgroup on the left. If its cumulative multiplier is $q^h$, the opposite target lattice is forced to be $q^{-h}(\Lambda_j')^\vee$. Consequently a chain of quotient choices is precisely a chain of selected overlattices, as in spherical convolution. The opposite lattices, polarizations, and transported markings agree for the same composite. The numbers of branches landing in each target component are locally constant on a connected source. Thus the component basepoint translations cancel when <a href="#eq:hecke-albanese-differences" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-albanese-differences">[eq:hecke-albanese-differences]</a> is composed.

Consider the central position with all selected exponents equal to one and multiplier exponent two. It is quotient by $B[q]$; under the multiplication-by-$q$ identification its target has the same polarization and action and marking $q\eta_N$. It is therefore a finite-order marking automorphism, since $q$ is invertible modulo $N$. Adding a sufficiently large power of this position to any integer exponent position puts it in <a href="#eq:positive-hecke-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-hecke-range">[eq:positive-hecke-range]</a>. Define its operator by clearing that power with the inverse marking automorphism. The positive convolution identity shows that this definition is independent of the clearing power. This gives the full rational spherical action. The spherical algebra is commutative, as follows also from the Satake isomorphism (Gross 1998, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Gross1998](#ref-Gross1998)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:frobenius-neighbors"></a>
#### A Frobenius polynomial from ordinary neighbors

The Hecke action is now algebraic. We next relate its reduction to Frobenius by counting quotient isogenies at ordinary points of the universal family. This calculation depends on the reference three-space and its Lie ranks; it is independent of the theta multiplicity line.

For $i=1,2,3$, let $T_i$ be the full moduli Hecke operator with $i$ active exponents equal to one, all other selected exponents zero, and multiplier exponent zero. These are the active minuscule cosets in the full similitude algebra. They need not be positive in <a href="#eq:positive-hecke-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-hecke-range">[eq:positive-hecke-range]</a>; Lemma <a href="#lem:hecke-albanese" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:hecke-albanese">21</a> defines them by central clearing.

Let $C_0$ be the central position with multiplier exponent one and all active exponents zero. On a nonactive selected block, take all three exponents to be one if its Lie rank is three, and zero if its Lie rank is zero. Put <a id="eq:integral-D-operators"></a>$$\label{eq:integral-D-operators}
D_i=C_0^iT_i,\qquad 1\leq i\leq3.$$ Here $D_i$ has multiplier exponent $i$, active exponents zero or one, and nonactive selected exponents zero or $i$. It is a positive raw operator, hence an integral homomorphism of $A_S$. The quotient branches defining $D_i$ can therefore be studied by specializing the isogenies constructed in Subsection <a href="#subsec:algebraic-hecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:algebraic-hecke">5.3</a>.

Let $\widetilde A_S/\mathbb{F}_q$ be the good reduction at the fixed completion of $K$, and let $\Phi$ be its $q$-power Frobenius endomorphism. The $K$-defined integral raw Hecke homomorphisms extend over $\mathbb{Z}_q$ by the Néron property; rational combinations extend after tensoring the endomorphism ring with $\mathbb{Q}$. Their reductions are defined over $\mathbb{F}_q$, so they commute with $\Phi$. Use the same letters $D_i$ for these reductions.

<a id="stmt-prop-albanese-congruence"></a>
<div id="prop:albanese-congruence" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial"></a>
**Proposition 22**. *In the integral endomorphism ring of $\widetilde A_S$, $$\label{eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial}
\Phi^3-D_1\Phi^2+qD_2\Phi-q^3D_3=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-albanese-congruence"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-albanese-congruence</summary>

*Proof.* Let $k=\overline{\mathbb{F}_q}$, and let $x$ be an ordinary geometric point of the special fiber of $S$. Its active $q$-divisible group has the canonical splitting $$G_w=M_0\oplus E_0,$$ where $M_0$ is multiplicative of height one and $E_0$ is étale of height two. Over the perfect field $k$, the reduced subgroup at each finite torsion level is the canonical étale part; it is preserved by the endomorphism idempotents. Each nonactive selected block is wholly multiplicative or wholly étale according to its Lie rank.

Three quotient constructions will account for the neighbors. Write $X_0$ for the quotient with active kernel $M_0[q]$ and multiplier $q$, $Y_0$ for the sum of quotients with active kernel a line $\ell\subset E_0[q]$ and multiplier $q$, and $Z_0$ for the quotient with active kernel $E_0[q]$ and multiplier $q^2$. For multiplier $q^h$, prescribe the connected $q^h$-torsion on every nonactive selected block and the forced annihilator on every opposite block. We will prove that these quotients define full tuples of $S$, that $X_0$ is Frobenius, and that the specialized neighbor sums are $$D_1:\ X_0+qY_0,\qquad
D_2:\ X_0Y_0+q^2Z_0,\qquad
D_3:\ X_0Z_0.$$ The displayed formulas are the identities to establish; at this point the quotient constructions have not yet been identified with operations on $S$. Once they are, their cancellation will give the asserted polynomial on Albanese differences.

*Specialization multiplicities.* Lift $x$, using smoothness, to $R=W(k)$, and write $\mathcal G_w$ for the lifted active $q$-divisible group. The ring $R$ is a complete henselian discrete valuation ring. In the lifted active $q$-torsion, the $q^2$ open-and-closed special label components lift uniquely by lifting idempotents of its finite coordinate algebra. Each lifted component is finite flat of rank $q$. The identity-specializing component is a subgroup, and multiplication respects the lifted labels by uniqueness. Thus there is an exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal H^0
\longrightarrow\mathcal G_w[q]
\longrightarrow(\mathbb{F}_q^2)_R\longrightarrow0$$ with $\mathcal H^0$ finite flat of rank $q$. No splitting of this sequence over $R$ is asserted.

Pass to a finite extension of $R$ over which the generic torsion is split. Its active fiber is a three-dimensional $\mathbb{F}_q$-space with distinguished line $\mathcal H^0_{\mathrm{gen}}$. For a fixed $b$-dimensional subspace of the label quotient, the generic subspaces disjoint from that line and mapping onto the label subspace are its graph lifts. They form a torsor under the $b$-dimensional space of maps to the line, so there are $q^b$ of them. A generic subspace containing the line is the unique full inverse image of its label subspace.

The schematic closure of a generic subgroup in the finite flat torsion scheme is finite torsion-free over the new DVR, hence flat. It is still a subgroup, since the subgroup identities extend from the generic fiber on flat schemes. Intersection with a label component is an idempotent direct summand of its coordinate algebra, so its rank is its generic rank. A graph has rank one on each of its labels. A finite flat rank-one algebra over a local ring is that ring, and its special fiber is the unique reduced point of that label component. All graph lifts over a label subspace therefore specialize to the same étale subgroup of $E_0[q]$. A subspace containing the distinguished line fills the corresponding rank-$q$ components and specializes to $M_0[q]$ plus that étale subgroup.

The active kernels of $D_i$ are $i$-dimensional subspaces of the generic $q$-torsion. The preceding count gives: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{operator}&\text{special active kernel}&\text{multiplicity}\\ \hline
D_1&M_0[q]&1\\
   &\ell\subset E_0[q],\ \dim_{\mathbb{F}_q}\ell=1&q\text{ for each }\ell\\
D_2&M_0[q]\oplus\ell,\ \dim_{\mathbb{F}_q}\ell=1&1\text{ for each }\ell\\
   &E_0[q]&q^2\\
D_3&M_0[q]\oplus E_0[q]&1 .
\end{array}$$ In the first two cases the total number is $q^2+q+1$: there are $q+1$ étale lines. The nonactive selected kernels are uniquely their connected $q^i$-torsion for $D_i$.

*Full tuples and Frobenius.* We now show that the counted kernels give targets in $S$, and identify the resulting quotient operations. Properness of the model of $S$ extends each generic neighbor over a finite valuation-ring extension. The universal source and target are abelian schemes over that new DVR. Apply the Néron mapping property there, anew, to extend the generic isogeny (Bosch, Lütkebohmert, and Raynaud 1990, vol. 21, sec. 1.2, Proposition 8) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990](#ref-BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990)]. The polarization identity extends by separatedness. On each fiber the kernel of the extension is contained in the finite kernel of $f^\vee\lambda'f=[q^i]\lambda$, so it has finite kernel and is an isogeny between equal-dimensional abelian varieties. Since source and target polarizations have the same degree, its degree is $q^{3si}$ on every fiber. Properness and fiberwise quasi-finiteness make the extension finite. Fiberwise flatness of isogenies between the smooth fibers and the relative flatness criterion make it flat; see (Bosch, Lütkebohmert, and Raynaud 1990, vol. 21, sec. 7.3, Lemma 1 and Proposition 6; §2.4, Proposition 2) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990](#ref-BoschLutkebohmertRaynaud1990)]. Its finite flat kernel is therefore the schematic closure of its generic kernel.

Finite-locally-free Cartier duality commutes with base change. For a subgroup $H\subset G$ in a finite group with a perfect pairing, the annihilator is $$\operatorname{ann}(H)
 =\ker(G^D\longrightarrow H^D)=(G/H)^D.$$ It is finite locally free and commutes with the above specialization. Thus the opposite generic kernels specialize to the annihilators of the selected kernels just counted. The corresponding nonactive kernels are already prescribed. Equal total kernels give canonically isomorphic abelian quotients. Their descended $\mathcal O_E$-actions and transported markings are unique, and their polarizations are unique by cancelling $f$ in $f^\vee\lambda'f=q^i\lambda$. The model retains these full tuples, so equal quotients are equal target points of $S$. In particular every counted kernel has such a target. It is ordinary, since ordinariness is invariant under isogeny.

The constructions $X_0,Y_0,Z_0$ are therefore operations on the ordinary points of the same full moduli space. We use them as multiset operations on points; no global special-fiber algebraic correspondence is required.

The operation $X_0$ is the $q$-power Frobenius on full tuples. Indeed its total kernel is the connected $q$-torsion on every block. On the active pair, duality pairs the multiplicative part with the opposite étale part and the étale part with the opposite multiplicative part, so the annihilator of $M_0[q]$ is exactly the opposite connected part; the pure nonactive pairs have the same property. The total kernel is therefore the kernel of relative Frobenius $F_B:B\to B^{(q)}$. Naturality of the polarization and Frobenius–Verschiebung duality give $$F_B^\vee\lambda^{(q)}F_B
 =V_{B^\vee}F_{B^\vee}\lambda=[q]\lambda.$$ Here $V_{B^\vee}$ denotes Verschiebung. The action on the target is $\iota_E^{(q)}$. For the constant label group $T=(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{6s}$, naturality gives $$F_B\eta_N=\eta_N^{(q)}F_T.$$ Relative Frobenius on $T$ fixes its labels, and $F_B$ is an isomorphism on $N$-torsion. Thus the transported marking is exactly the twisted marking, with no multiplication-by-$q$ marking change. Since the model and universal data are defined over $\mathbb{F}_q$, this is its coordinate-$q$-power operation on geometric points, not its inverse.

Frobenius commutes with $Y_0$ and with $Z_0$ as multiset operations. For any of their quotient maps $f:B\to B'$, $$F_{B'}f=f^{(q)}F_B.$$ Frobenius is an isomorphism on the étale part and permutes its line choices bijectively; this square also respects the polarizations, actions, and markings. On the active block the composite $X_0Y_0$ has kernel $M_0[q]\oplus\ell$ with multiplier $q^2$, and $X_0Z_0$ has kernel $M_0[q]\oplus E_0[q]$ with multiplier $q^3$.

For clarity, the opposite kernels of these composites are indeed forced by their selected kernels and multipliers. On the $q$-divisible groups, write $G,G'$ for source and target and define the polarization adjoint $f^\dagger=\lambda^{-1}f^D\lambda'$; the polarizations are isomorphisms there because their degrees are prime to $q$. For a composite with multiplier $q^h$, $$f^\dagger f=[q^h],\qquad ff^\dagger=[q^h].$$ The second identity follows by cancellation of the fppf epimorphism $f$. As fppf subgroup sheaves, $\ker f=f^\dagger(G'[q^h])$. For $u,v\in G'[q^h]$, adjunction gives $$e_{q^h}\bigl(f^\dagger u,f^\dagger v\bigr)
 =e'_{q^h}\bigl(u,ff^\dagger v\bigr)=1,$$ so the image is isotropic in $G[q^h]$. The endomorphism idempotents split its kernel into conjugate-block parts. If $K_j,K_{\bar j}$ are the parts in one conjugate pair of height-three blocks, then $$K_{\bar j}\subseteq K_j^\perp,\qquad
\operatorname{rank}(K_j)\operatorname{rank}(K_{\bar j})
 \leq q^{3h}.$$ Cartier duality gives equal kernel ranks for $f$ and $f^\dagger$. Their composite has kernel rank $q^{6sh}$, so $\operatorname{rank}\ker f=q^{3sh}$. Multiplying the pairwise inequalities gives equality, and hence equality in each pair. Thus $K_{\bar j}=K_j^\perp$. For $X_0Z_0$, for example, the annihilator of the active $q$-torsion in $q^3$-torsion is the opposite block’s $q^2$-torsion. The pure nonactive connected kernels likewise compose to connected $q^h$-torsion. Uniqueness of the polarizations and markings now identifies the composite targets with the counted targets. We have proved the multiset identities <a id="eq:ordinary-neighbor-counts"></a>$$\label{eq:ordinary-neighbor-counts}
\begin{aligned}
D_1&:\quad X_0+qY_0,\\
D_2&:\quad X_0Y_0+q^2Z_0,\\
D_3&:\quad X_0Z_0.
\end{aligned}$$

*The integral Albanese identity.* The neighbor counts are now identities of multisets of full tuples. We pass from them to endomorphisms by applying the Albanese difference formula. Let $\mathcal O$ be the set of ordinary $k$-points of the union of components and extend $X_0,Y_0,Z_0$ linearly to the free abelian group $\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal O]$. Define $$\alpha:\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal O]\longrightarrow\widetilde A_S(k),
\qquad \alpha([x])=a_{j(x)}(x).$$ The component Albanese maps reduce over $\mathbb{F}_q$, so $\Phi\alpha=\alpha X_0$. For ordinary $x,y$ in one source component, lift them and specialize the integral identity <a href="#eq:hecke-albanese-differences" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-albanese-differences">[eq:hecke-albanese-differences]</a>. With $$N_1=X_0+qY_0,\qquad
N_2=X_0Y_0+q^2Z_0,\qquad
N_3=X_0Z_0,$$ it gives $$D_i\bigl(a_j(x)-a_j(y)\bigr)
 =\alpha\bigl(N_i([x]-[y])\bigr).$$ This is an equality in the actual group of geometric points, with all branch multiplicities retained. No rationalization of a finite-field point group is used.

Frobenius preserves each component and its ordinary locus. Applying the same formula to $X_0^m x,X_0^m y$ shows that the endomorphism in <a href="#eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial">[eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial]</a> on this difference is $$\alpha\!\left(
(X_0^3-N_1X_0^2+qN_2X_0-q^3N_3)([x]-[y])
\right).$$ The operator inside this expression vanishes: $$\begin{aligned}
&X_0^3-N_1X_0^2+qN_2X_0-q^3N_3\\
&\qquad=-qY_0X_0^2+qX_0Y_0X_0
        +q^3Z_0X_0-q^3X_0Z_0=0.
\end{aligned}$$ Only the two proved commutations with $X_0$ are used. Ordinary points are dense in every smooth component by Lemma <a href="#lem:good-ordinary-primes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:good-ordinary-primes">20</a>. Therefore the morphism obtained by applying the endomorphism to $a_j(x)-a_j(y)$ vanishes on all of the product of that component with itself. The finite-sum generation in the same lemma makes the endomorphism zero on each Albanese factor. It is zero on $\widetilde A_S$ after base change to $k$; faithful base change gives the integral identity over $\mathbb{F}_q$. ◻

</details>

For the cohomological application, we record the Frobenius convention. On $H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde A_S/\mathbb{Z}_q)[1/q]$, crystalline Frobenius is the pullback $\varphi=\Phi^*$ of this $q$-power endomorphism: it is defined by absolute-Frobenius pullback, and the Witt Frobenius on $W(\mathbb{F}_q)=\mathbb{Z}_q$ is the identity because $q$ is prime (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tag 07N0) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject)]. In degree-one cohomology pullback is additive in abelian homomorphisms. It reverses composition, but the displayed endomorphisms commute. Thus <a href="#eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial">[eq:albanese-frobenius-polynomial]</a> gives the same operator polynomial with $\varphi$ and the Hecke pullbacks, not a reciprocal polynomial. This is the identity used in Proposition <a href="#prop:active-hodge-types" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:active-hodge-types">28</a>.

<a id="sec:cm-sources"></a>
### CM sources of the theta one-forms

We now prove Theorem <a href="#thm:cm-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm-extraction">18</a>, which identifies the rational CM sources of the theta classes. Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">5</a> constructed an algebraic Hecke action and a cubic relation with Frobenius. We calculate the active Hecke characters of the theta classes, insert them into that relation, and use degree-one Weil weight to isolate one Frobenius slope on every full character space. The Hecke projectors preserve the Hodge filtration, so these slopes determine the Hodge types of all scalar conjugates. Scalar descent will then give the required rational Hodge maps.

Retain the fields, moduli space, Albanese product, and Hecke operators of Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">5</a>. In particular, the split good prime $q$ was chosen after fixing the finite theta inputs and their common principal level.

<a id="subsec:active-parameter"></a>
#### The active spherical parameter

We calculate the active Hecke characters in two steps. First we identify the geometric Hecke action on theta inputs as the inverse spherical action. We then compute that action on local inputs fixed by $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$, which form a cyclic module for a dilation operator. Rational scalar covariance will make its eigenvalues algebraic and encode the compact-place signs in their $q$-adic valuations. A lattice count then identifies the spherical parameter, with the raw Hecke normalization fixed.

Let $\mathcal H_w^0$ be the rational subalgebra supported on positions with multiplier exponent zero, every nonactive selected block trivial, and active exponent sum zero. It is the spherical algebra of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta"></a>
<div id="lem:active-hecke-theta" class="lemma">

**Lemma 23**. *Every double coset in $\mathcal H_w^0$ has all its branches within each component of $S$. On the original ball component, its pullback action on theta classes is the inverse spherical action on the active local Weil input: a local double coset acts through its inverse.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-active-hecke-theta</summary>

*Proof.* As a quasi-isogeny, such a position leaves the rational polarized period fixed. It changes only the active lattice and its opposite dual, keeping the marking and all other local data. This is also the description obtained by clearing the central shift in Lemma <a href="#lem:hecke-albanese" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:hecke-albanese">21</a> and undoing its marking change. A determinant-exponent-zero lattice position has a representative in $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q)$. Adjusting determinant units in Smith bases shows that its double cosets and branches are exactly those for $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q)/\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$.

For each component apply strong approximation to the special-unitary group of its rational Hermitian space. This group is simply connected and absolutely almost simple over $F$, and its factor at $\mu$ is noncompact. Strong approximation away from all infinite places therefore makes its $F$-points dense in its finite adelic points (Milne 2005, Theorem 4.16) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[MilneShimura2017](#ref-MilneShimura2017)]. Put $K_q=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$. Write $\Lambda^{(j)}$ for the marked lattice of the component under consideration. For a branch with new active lattice $g^{-1}\Lambda_w^{(j)}$, write $(\Lambda^{(j)})'$ for the full changed lattice and choose a rational representative $\gamma$ whose active component lies in $K_qg$ and whose other finite components are integral and marking-preserving. Then $\gamma(\Lambda^{(j)})'=\Lambda^{(j)}$, and $\gamma$ identifies the target tuple with the transformed negative period line in the same component.

On the original component, Lemmas <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a> and <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a> give $$\gamma^*\Theta\bigl(\omega_f(\gamma)\phi\bigr)=\Theta(\phi).$$ Thus fixed-input pullback by the branch acts through $\omega_f(\gamma)^{-1}$. Away from $w$ the chosen level fixes the input. At $w$ our good-prime choice makes it invariant under $K_q$; if $\gamma_w=kg$, its inverse action on that input is therefore $\omega_f(g^{-1})$. Summing the branches proves the inverse spherical formula. The sum is raw: no division by its degree and no additional power of $q$ occurs. ◻

</details>

We now compute this inverse spherical action on the local inputs.

At $w$, choose the Schrödinger position space to be the three-dimensional $w$-embedding summand, with lattice $\mathbb{Z}_q^3$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:unitary-splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:unitary-splitting">14</a>, the action of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q)$ is inverse linear substitution. Every finite input is a finite sum of tensors separating this place from the other finite places. Our choice of $q$ makes the input invariant under $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$; averaging a separated decomposition over that compact group gives one whose local factors are invariant.

The invariant Schwartz functions are radial: $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$ is transitive on vectors with a fixed minimum coordinate valuation. Local constancy at zero and compact support therefore make this space the span of $$b_j=\mathbf 1_{q^j\mathbb{Z}_q^3},\qquad j\in\mathbb{Z}.$$ The functions $b_j$ are linearly independent. Define the normalized dilation <a id="eq:radial-dilation"></a>$$\label{eq:radial-dilation}
(\mathcal D_q b)(x)=q^{3/2}b(q^{-1}x).$$ Then $\mathcal D_qb_j=q^{3/2}b_{j+1}$, so the radial space is a cyclic module for $\mathbb{C}[\mathcal D_q,\mathcal D_q^{-1}]$.

<a id="stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation"></a>
<div id="lem:scalar-radial-relation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:radial-scalar-weight"></a>
**Lemma 24**. *Fix the input at all finite places other than $w$. There are $M>0$, an element $r\in E^\times$, and a nonzero algebraic number $\omega$ such that $$r\bar r=1,\qquad
\operatorname{val}_{w}(r)=M,\qquad
\operatorname{val}_{\bar w}(r)=-M,$$ with zero valuation at every other finite prime, and the map from radial inputs to theta one-form classes factors through $\mathcal D_q^M=\omega$. In the covariance convention of Lemma <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a>, $$\label{eq:radial-scalar-weight}
\omega^2=\zeta\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma}\tau(r)^{-3d(\tau)}$$ for a root of unity $\zeta$. Every root of $X^M-\omega$ has absolute value one under every complex embedding.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-scalar-radial-relation</summary>

*Proof.* Principalize a positive power $w^m=(a)$ and put $r=a/\bar a$. It has norm one and the required valuations with $M=m$. At every other finite place it is a unit. Powers of an element of a compact unit group enter every prescribed open subgroup: its image in the finite quotient by that subgroup has finite order. Smoothness of the bare local Weil representation gives an open stabilizer of the fixed input in the metaplectic group; its image meets the scalar unit group in an open subgroup. Replacing $r$ by a common positive power therefore places it in the stabilizers needed at the finitely many exceptional places of the fixed input; replace $M$ accordingly.

Multiplication by $r$ on $V\otimes_E P$ preserves its $F$-symplectic form because $r\bar r=1$. We use its *bare rational symplectic* covariance, with paired metaplectic lifts, not the value of a chosen unitary splitting character. For a transformation $h$ of a local position space, the normalized linear action is $$(\rho(h)b)(x)=|\det h|_q^{-1/2}b(h^{-1}x),$$ up to the finite Weil phase of the bare lift. The linear-subgroup formula, rational summation invariance, and the double-cover control are in (Weil 1964, I, no. 13; III, no. 41; IV, nos. 43–45) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[Weil1964](#ref-Weil1964)]. At $w$, the position action is the scalar $r_w$, of valuation $M$. Its unit part fixes a radial function, so the finite action there is $\mathcal D_q^M$, up to a root of unity. At almost every other place the input is the unramified lattice vector and $r$ is integral. At the remaining places the chosen power fixes the input under a suitable bare lift; the paired lift differs only by its finite phase. Their product is again a root of unity. An arbitrary lift in the circle pushout would not justify this conclusion, which is why the bare rational action is used here.

At infinity, multiplication by $r$ acts on the $\tau$-embedding summand as the scalar $\tau(r)$: this is the defining scalar action after tensoring the $E$-space through $\tau$. On the $c\tau$-summand it acts as $\tau(\bar r)=\tau(r)^{-1}$. It consequently fixes every chosen positive Lagrangian and the point of $D$. In the identity $g e_Z=e_{gZ}J$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:theta-tensor-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:theta-tensor-law">12</a>, $J$ is this action on the homology filtration. The squared scalar on the projected first tensor is the scalar on $\det\mathcal F_P$ times the square of the scalar on its projected $c$-summand. At $\mu$, the filtration has scalar exponents $1,-1,-1$. Its determinant contributes $-1$, and the squared projected conjugate factor contributes $-2$. The total is $-3$. At a compact pair, the selected full rank-three summand contributes $-3d(\tau)$, with no projected tangent factor. Thus the squared infinite scalar is $\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma}\tau(r)^{-3d(\tau)}$. This calculation includes the scalar action on the determinant’s constant line, which is trivial only on $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(V)$.

The covariance formula has the finite transformed input on its left and the tensor action on its right. Since $r$ fixes the period, it gives $\Theta(\mathcal D_q^M b)=\omega\Theta(b)$, with the finite phase absorbed in $\omega$, and its square is exactly <a href="#eq:radial-scalar-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-scalar-weight">[eq:radial-scalar-weight]</a>. This equality of one-forms implies the asserted equality of cohomology classes.

The right side of <a href="#eq:radial-scalar-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-scalar-weight">[eq:radial-scalar-weight]</a> is algebraic, so $\omega$ is algebraic. Every complex embedding of $E$ sends $r$ to a number of modulus one, since $E$ is CM and $r\bar r=1$. Applying any scalar conjugation to the displayed identity shows that every complex conjugate of $\omega$ has modulus one. The same is then true of each root of $X^M-\omega$. ◻

</details>

For a finite separated sum of inputs, take the common stabilizers of all its other-place factors in this proof. The same $r,M,\omega$ then works for that sum.

We use the half-modulus Satake convention, namely the parameter attached to normalized parabolic induction. We do not apply the additional central Hecke/Tate shift of Gross’s Section 8. In particular the trivial representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2$ has parameter $\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}\}$. On $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$, a parameter is an unordered triple of nonzero scalars modulo a common scalar. We say that an active character occurs in a theta class when its simultaneous generalized eigenspace in the finite-dimensional Hecke span has a nonzero projection of that class.

<a id="stmt-prop-theta-satake"></a>
<div id="prop:theta-satake" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:active-satake-triple"></a>
<a id="eq:active-conjugate-valuations"></a>
**Proposition 25**. *Every active spherical character occurring in a theta class of the multiplicity line $P$ has half-modulus projective parameter $$\label{eq:active-satake-triple}
\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e\},$$ where $e=e_0^{-1}$ for a root $e_0^M=\omega$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-radial-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-radial-relation">24</a>. It is algebraic and all its complex conjugates have modulus one. For every $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})$, $$\label{eq:active-conjugate-valuations}
\nu_\iota(\sigma e)
 =-\frac32d\bigl((\sigma|_E)^{-1}\bigr)
 =-\frac32v(\sigma|_E).$$ A scalar-conjugate active character has a representative of the form <a href="#eq:active-satake-triple" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-satake-triple">[eq:active-satake-triple]</a> with third entry $e'=\pm\sigma e$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-theta-satake"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-theta-satake</summary>

*Proof.* The active spherical action commutes with $\mathcal D_q$. The radial Laurent module modulo $\mathcal D_q^M-\omega$ is a direct sum of one-dimensional spaces $\mathcal D_q=e_0$, because $X^M-\omega$ has distinct nonzero roots. It suffices to compute the spherical character on one of them. This decomposition concerns the radial quotient only; it asserts no semisimplicity for the ambient cohomological Hecke action.

<a id="eq:radial-minuscule-actions"></a>
For this computation, extend the substitutions to $$\rho(g)b(x)=|\det g|_q^{-1/2}b(g^{-1}x),
\qquad g\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3(\mathbb{Q}_q),$$ and put $K_3=\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3(\mathbb{Z}_q)$. This is a computational extension of the active $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$-action, not a claim of theta equivariance for the full moduli similitude group. Let $\mathsf T_i$ be the raw double coset of $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(q,\ldots,q,1,\ldots,1)$, with $i$ entries $q$, and $\operatorname{vol}(K_3)=1$. Its right cosets correspond to sublattices between $q\mathbb{Z}_q^3$ and $\mathbb{Z}_q^3$ of index $q^i$. For $i=1,2$ there are $q^2+q+1$ such lattices. The numbers containing a fixed primitive vector are respectively $q+1$ and $1$.
