Since $\rho(g)b_0$ is $q^{i/2}$ times the characteristic function of its sublattice, we obtain $$\label{eq:radial-minuscule-actions}
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf T_1b_0&=q^{1/2}\bigl((q+1)b_0+q^2b_1\bigr),\\
\mathsf T_2b_0&=q\bigl(b_0+(q^2+q)b_1\bigr),\\
\mathsf T_3b_0&=\mathcal D_qb_0.
\end{aligned}$$ For example, vectors in $q\mathbb{Z}_q^3$ belong to every sublattice, while a primitive vector is tested by its nonzero class in $\mathbb{F}_q^3$; the displayed counts are the numbers of the corresponding planes or lines containing that class.

On the quotient $\mathcal D_q=e_0$ we have $b_1=q^{-3/2}e_0b_0$. Substitution into <a href="#eq:radial-minuscule-actions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-minuscule-actions">[eq:radial-minuscule-actions]</a> gives the values $$q\,e_1(e_0,q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}),\qquad
q\,e_2(e_0,q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}),\qquad
e_3(e_0,q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}),$$ where $e_i$ is the $i$-th elementary symmetric polynomial. In the half-modulus Satake isomorphism the raw minuscule cosets have transforms $q e_1,q e_2,e_3$; these generate the spherical algebra, with $e_3$ invertible. See (Gross 1998, Equation (3.14) and §6) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[Gross1998](#ref-Gross1998)], where the $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n$ formula is attributed to Tamagawa. The character just computed therefore has parameter $\{e_0,q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}\}$.

The geometric action on theta inputs is inverse by Lemma <a href="#lem:active-hecke-theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:active-hecke-theta">23</a>. Inversion sends the half-modulus parameter to its reciprocal, without a degree factor. One can check this on the same generators: $\mathsf T_1^\vee=\mathsf T_3^{-1}\mathsf T_2$, $\mathsf T_2^\vee=\mathsf T_3^{-1}\mathsf T_1$, and $\mathsf T_3^\vee=\mathsf T_3^{-1}$, where $f^\vee(g)=f(g^{-1})$. Their transforms are the corresponding elementary symmetric functions of the reciprocal triple. After interchanging the first two entries, the active projective parameter is therefore $\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e_0^{-1}\}$.

The determinant-zero $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3$ double cosets agree as in Lemma <a href="#lem:active-hecke-theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:active-hecke-theta">23</a>. Their Satake unipotent integrals and modulus factors agree as well. The half-sum of positive roots is $(1,0,-1)$, integral on the relevant cocharacter lattices; the Satake comparison is consequently defined over rational coefficients (Gross 1998, Proposition 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[Gross1998](#ref-Gross1998)]. Scalar conjugation of an active numerical character therefore conjugates its projective parameter. It may change the two entries $q^{1/2},q^{-1/2}$ by their common sign. Removing that sign projectively leaves third entry $\pm\sigma e$.

It remains to calculate the valuation. Put $g=\sigma|_E$. By the definition of the prime labels, $$\nu_\iota(\sigma\tau(r))
 =\operatorname{val}_{\mathfrak p_{g\tau}}(r).$$ This is nonzero only when $g\tau=1$ or $c$. Among the representatives $\Sigma$, the contributing one is $g^{-1}$ with valuation $-M$, or $cg^{-1}$ with valuation $M$. Here $c$ is central in $G_E$, and oddness of $d$ gives in either case $$\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma}d(\tau)\nu_\iota(\sigma\tau(r))
   =-M d(g^{-1}).$$ Taking valuations in <a href="#eq:radial-scalar-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-scalar-weight">[eq:radial-scalar-weight]</a> and in $e_0^M=\omega$ yields $\nu_\iota(\sigma e_0)=\tfrac32d(g^{-1})$. Since $e=e_0^{-1}$ and $d(g^{-1})=v(g)$, this proves <a href="#eq:active-conjugate-valuations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-conjugate-valuations">[eq:active-conjugate-valuations]</a>. The unit-modulus assertion follows from Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-radial-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-radial-relation">24</a>. ◻

</details>

The active character is now known, including the valuation of every scalar conjugate. The full Hecke character may still multiply its three Satake entries by a common scalar. The following finite-order relations will control that scalar when we apply the Frobenius polynomial of Proposition <a href="#prop:albanese-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:albanese-congruence">22</a>.

<a id="stmt-lem-central-finite-order"></a>
<div id="lem:central-finite-order" class="lemma">

**Lemma 26**. *The central operators $T_3$ and $C_0$ have finite order on the full tuple space $S$ and on $A_S$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-central-finite-order"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-central-finite-order</summary>

*Proof.* Each central position has a unique lattice choice. For $T_3$, use the norm-one element $r$ constructed in Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-radial-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-radial-relation">24</a>; only its valuations and norm are needed here. The scalar $r$ takes the $T_3^M$-target lattice back to the source lattice on the active and opposite blocks. It preserves every polarized period because it is an $E$-scalar of norm one. Its denominators are supported at $q$, so a further power is one on the $N$-marking. That power of $T_3$ is the identity on every tuple.

For $C_0$, let $I$ be the product of the primes where its full exponent is one. There is exactly one from each conjugate pair, including $\bar w$, and $I\bar I=(q)$. Choose $I^m=(a)$. Then $$a\bar a=q^m u$$ for a totally positive unit $u\in\mathcal O_F^\times$. The scalar $b=a^2/u$ has exactly the valuations of $I^{2m}$ and satisfies $$b\bar b=q^{2m}.$$ It identifies the corresponding powered target lattice with the source, with exactly the required polarization multiplier. A further power fixes the marking. This argument works on every rational Hermitian component because an $E$-scalar preserves each of its embedding filtrations. Thus a power of $C_0$ is also the identity on the full tuple space. The induced Albanese homomorphisms have finite order as well. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:slopes-to-types"></a>
#### From Frobenius slopes to Hodge types

We now combine the Frobenius polynomial with the theta parameters to determine the Hodge types of the relevant Hecke spaces. The polynomial will force a single Newton slope on each full generalized character space. To turn this into a Hodge type, we also need its projector to preserve the Hodge filtration. We first construct these projectors and explain the filtered argument.

<a id="eq:active-generalized-space"></a>
Let $\mathbb T$ be the image of the full spherical Hecke algebra in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\mathbb{Q}}H^1(A_S,\mathbb{Q})$. It is a finite-dimensional commutative $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra; its active subalgebra is the image of $\mathcal H_w^0$. A numerical character is a $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra homomorphism $\chi:\mathbb T\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$. Inside a character value, the symbol of an endomorphism denotes its degree-one pullback. After a finite extension of algebraic coefficients, $\mathbb T$ is a product of local Artinian algebras. Its product idempotents $p_\chi$ give the simultaneous generalized character spaces $$H_\chi=p_\chi H^1(A_S,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}).$$ If $\eta$ is a character of the active subalgebra, its generalized space is $$\label{eq:active-generalized-space}
H^{\mathrm{act}}_\eta
=\bigoplus_{\chi|_{\mathrm{act}}=\eta}H_\chi.$$ Indeed the image of the active idempotent in a local factor of the full algebra is either zero or one, as decided by its residue character. Thus the sum includes every full extension of $\eta$ and all of its nilpotent parts. No semisimplicity of the Hecke operators is assumed.

Each Hecke operator is induced by a rational endomorphism of $A_S$ defined over $K$. In degree-one Betti, de Rham, and crystalline cohomology, pullback is additive in abelian homomorphisms: in the Künneth decomposition of $H^1(A\times A)$, pullback by addition has both components equal to the identity, as restriction to the two axes shows. Pullback reverses composition, but the Hecke endomorphisms commute. Pullback on Betti $H^1$ is faithful by Lemma <a href="#lem:weight-one-maps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weight-one-maps">3</a>, so polynomial relations in $\mathbb T$ are also relations among the corresponding endomorphisms. The same degree-one functoriality preserves those relations in de Rham and crystalline cohomology. Thus the $p_\chi$ remain idempotents in these degree-one realizations after extension of coefficients. They need not have rational coefficients; what matters is that they are algebraic-coefficient polynomials in extending endomorphisms.

The following lemma applies to precisely these projected summands. Its use of both complementary projectors is what turns weak admissibility into equality of the Hodge and Newton numbers on each summand.

<a id="stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands"></a>
<div id="lem:filtered-hecke-summands" class="lemma">

**Lemma 27**. *Let $\mathcal A/\mathbb{Z}_q$ be an abelian scheme with generic fiber $A$, and put $$D_A=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(A/\mathbb{Q}_q).$$ Equip $D_A$ with its Hodge filtration and its good-reduction crystalline Frobenius $\varphi_A$. Then $D_A$ is weakly admissible, its filtration has only indices $0$ and $1$, and endomorphisms extending over $\mathbb{Z}_q$ preserve the filtration and commute with $\varphi_A$.*

*Let $L/\mathbb{Q}_q$ be finite. Suppose that an $L$-linear idempotent $p$ on $D_A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_q}L$ preserves the filtration and commutes with $\varphi_A\otimes1$. If every Frobenius slope on its image is $t\in\{0,1\}$, then the induced filtration satisfies $$F^1\bigl(p(D_A\otimes L)\bigr)=
\begin{cases}
0,&t=0,\\
p(D_A\otimes L),&t=1.
\end{cases}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-filtered-hecke-summands</summary>

*Proof.* Good-reduction crystalline comparison identifies $$D_{\mathrm{cris}}\!
 \left(H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(A_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_q},\mathbb{Q}_q)\right)
\simeq
H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal A_{\mathbb{F}_q}/\mathbb{Z}_q)[1/q]$$ and identifies the filtered de Rham realization with $D_A$. These identifications are functorial for extending endomorphisms and compatible with Frobenius and filtration (Bhatt, Morrow, and Scholze 2018, Theorem 1.1(i), Theorem 12.1, and §12.2) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[BhattMorrowScholze2018](#ref-BhattMorrowScholze2018)].[^1] As in Subsection <a href="#subsec:frobenius-neighbors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:frobenius-neighbors">5.4</a>, crystalline Frobenius is the pullback of the special fiber’s $q$-power endomorphism: Witt Frobenius on $W(\mathbb{F}_q)=\mathbb{Z}_q$ is the identity (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tags 07N3 and 07N5) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject)]. Crystalline representations give weakly admissible filtered modules (Brinon and Conrad 2009, Theorem 9.3.4) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[BrinonConrad2009](#ref-BrinonConrad2009)]. The degree-one Hodge filtration has only indices $0$ and $1$, and functoriality gives the assertion about extending endomorphisms.

The extension by $L$ changes coefficients, not the geometric base field. With Frobenius acting trivially on this coefficient factor, the underlying $\mathbb{Q}_q$-filtered Frobenius module $$\bigl(D_A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_q}L,\ \varphi_A\otimes1,\
       F^iD_A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_q}L\bigr)$$ is a direct sum of $[L:\mathbb{Q}_q]$ copies of $D_A$, and is weakly admissible. Since $p$ and $1-p$ both preserve the filtration and commute with Frobenius, their images split it as filtered Frobenius modules. Weak admissibility gives $t_H\leq t_N$ on each image. The Hodge and Newton numbers are additive, and are equal for the whole module. They are therefore equal on each image separately.

Let $m=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_q}p(D_A\otimes L)$. A pure slope $t$ gives $t_N=tm$ after restriction of scalars: all $\mathbb{Q}_q$-conjugates of a $q$-adic algebraic number have the same valuation, including when $L/\mathbb{Q}_q$ is ramified. On the other hand, $$t_H=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_q}F^1\bigl(p(D_A\otimes L)\bigr),$$ because the only filtration indices are $0$ and $1$. Equality of these two numbers proves the assertion. The general coefficient formalism is also described in (Hellmann 2011, Proposition 2.17(ii) and Corollary 2.22) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[Hellmann2011](#ref-Hellmann2011)]. ◻

</details>

The requirement on the idempotent is essential. For example, $\varphi=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(1,q)$ on $\mathbb{Q}_qe_0\oplus\mathbb{Q}_qe_1$, with only filtration indices $0,1$ and $F^1=\langle e_0+e_1\rangle$, is weakly admissible, but its slope-one line has induced $F^1=0$. Its slope projector does not preserve the filtration. The Hecke projectors do preserve it, because they are formed from extending algebraic endomorphisms.

We can therefore obtain a pure Hodge type by proving that a full generalized Hecke space has slope zero or one. The finite-order central relation controls the common scalar in a full extension of the active projective parameter, and the Frobenius polynomial then determines the slope.

<a id="eq:satake-central-relation"></a>
Fix an active character $\eta$ with projective parameter $$\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e'\},$$ where $e'=e$ is supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-satake" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-satake">25</a>, or $e'=\pm\sigma e$ is the parameter of a scalar-conjugate character. Thus $e'$ is algebraic, all its complex conjugates have modulus one, and $\nu_\iota(e')\in\{-3/2,3/2\}$. For a full character $\chi$ extending $\eta$, its active $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3$ parameter is a scalar multiple of this triple. Absorb the numerical value of $C_0$ into that scalar and write the resulting triple as <a id="eq:full-satake-triple"></a>$$\label{eq:full-satake-triple}
(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3)
=b(q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e').$$ The determinant coordinate is the character value of $D_3=C_0^3T_3$. Lemma <a href="#lem:central-finite-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:central-finite-order">26</a> therefore gives $$\label{eq:satake-central-relation}
b^3e' \text{ is a root of unity}.$$ In particular $b$ is algebraic and every complex conjugate of $b$ has modulus one.

<a id="eq:raw-D-character"></a>
Write $e_i$ for the elementary symmetric polynomials in three variables. The raw minuscule double cosets, with the integral subgroup of volume one, have Satake transforms $q e_1,\ q e_2,\ e_3$; this is (Gross 1998, Equation (3.14) and §6) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[Gross1998](#ref-Gross1998)] for $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_3$. Since $D_i=C_0^iT_i$, absorption of the $C_0$-value into $b$ gives $$\label{eq:raw-D-character}
\chi(D_1)=q e_1(\alpha),\qquad
\chi(D_2)=q e_2(\alpha),\qquad
\chi(D_3)=e_3(\alpha).$$ These are numerical character values. On $H_\chi$ the operators may still have nilpotent parts.

<a id="stmt-prop-active-hodge-types"></a>
<div id="prop:active-hodge-types" class="proposition">

**Proposition 28**. *Let $\eta$ be an active numerical character with projective parameter $\{q^{1/2},q^{-1/2},e'\}$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-satake" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-satake">25</a> or by scalar conjugation, and put $$t_\eta=\frac12-\frac13\nu_\iota(e')\in\{0,1\}.$$ For every full numerical character $\chi$ extending $\eta$, the space $H_\chi\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}$ is contained in $H^{1,0}(A_S)$ if $t_\eta=1$, and in $H^{0,1}(A_S)$ if $t_\eta=0$. Consequently $H^{\mathrm{act}}_\eta\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}$ has the same pure Hodge type.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-active-hodge-types"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-active-hodge-types</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a full character $\chi$ extending $\eta$; if there is none, the assertion is vacuous. We first use Weil weight to select the Frobenius root. We then apply Lemma <a href="#lem:filtered-hecke-summands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:filtered-hecke-summands">27</a> and compare the resulting filtration ranks with those on the complex Betti realization.

At the fixed completion $K_v=\mathbb{Q}_q$, write $$D_S=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}((A_S)_{K_v}/\mathbb{Q}_q)$$ and let $\varphi$ be its crystalline Frobenius. If $\Phi$ is the $q$-power Frobenius endomorphism of $\widetilde A_S/\mathbb{F}_q$ used in Proposition <a href="#prop:albanese-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:albanese-congruence">22</a>, then $\varphi=\Phi^*$ under crystalline comparison. The Hecke endomorphisms extend over $\mathbb{Z}_q$; their reductions commute with $\Phi$, so their pullbacks commute with $\varphi$.

<a id="eq:cohomological-frobenius-polynomial"></a>
By additivity of pullback on abelian degree-one cohomology, the integral identity of Proposition <a href="#prop:albanese-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:albanese-congruence">22</a> gives $$\label{eq:cohomological-frobenius-polynomial}
\varphi^3-D_1^*\varphi^2+qD_2^*\varphi-q^3D_3^*=0.$$ Pullback reverses composition, but all operators in this formula commute.

Choose a prime $\ell\ne q$ and put $V_\ell\widetilde A_S=T_\ell\widetilde A_S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_\ell}\mathbb{Q}_\ell$. Weil’s theorem for abelian varieties over $\mathbb{F}_q$ gives $$P_\Phi(X)=\det\bigl(X-\Phi\mid V_\ell\widetilde A_S\bigr)\in\mathbb{Z}[X],$$ all of whose complex roots have absolute value $q^{1/2}$. The prime-to-$q$ Tate-module action on endomorphisms is faithful. Cayley–Hamilton therefore gives $P_\Phi(\Phi)=0$ in the endomorphism ring, and degree-one additivity gives $P_\Phi(\varphi)=0$. These statements hold for an arbitrary abelian variety over $\mathbb{F}_q$; see (Milne 1986, Lemma 12.2, Proposition 12.9, and Theorem 19.1(c) with its proof) <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[Milne1986AbelianVarieties](#ref-Milne1986AbelianVarieties)]. No CM or ordinary hypothesis on $\widetilde A_S$ is involved. This uses the $q$-power endomorphism and its pullback, not a reciprocal Galois-Frobenius convention.

Transport the algebraic coefficients of $p_\chi$ and $\chi$ by $\iota$. On the corresponding generalized summand of $D_S\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_q}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_q$, the commuting Hecke maps and $\varphi$ can be simultaneously triangularized. Equations <a href="#eq:cohomological-frobenius-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cohomological-frobenius-polynomial">[eq:cohomological-frobenius-polynomial]</a> and <a href="#eq:raw-D-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:raw-D-character">[eq:raw-D-character]</a> imply that every diagonal Frobenius eigenvalue is a root of $$X^3-qe_1(\alpha)X^2+q^2e_2(\alpha)X-q^3e_3(\alpha)
=\prod_{j=1}^3(X-q\alpha_j).$$ Thus the candidates, before applying $\iota$, are <a id="eq:frobenius-candidates"></a>$$\label{eq:frobenius-candidates}
bq^{3/2},\qquad bq^{1/2},\qquad qbe'.$$ Each is algebraic. If its image under $\iota$ is a root of the rational polynomial $P_\Phi$, the algebraic number itself is a root of $P_\Phi$. We may therefore apply the complex absolute-value condition. The three absolute values are respectively $$q^{3/2},\qquad q^{1/2},\qquad q,$$ because every complex conjugate of $b$ and $e'$ has modulus one. Only $bq^{1/2}$ remains.

<a id="eq:active-hodge-slope"></a>
The weight condition has now selected one Newton slope on the entire generalized summand. Equation <a href="#eq:satake-central-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:satake-central-relation">[eq:satake-central-relation]</a> gives $$\label{eq:active-hodge-slope}
\nu_\iota(bq^{1/2})
=\frac12-\frac13\nu_\iota(e')=t_\eta.$$ This conclusion concerns every Frobenius eigenvalue; Jordan blocks do not change it. Choose a finite extension $L/\mathbb{Q}_q$ containing the coefficients of the transported idempotent and numerical character. The idempotent $p_\chi$ is a polynomial in extending Hecke maps, so it preserves the Hodge filtration and commutes with $\varphi$. Lemma <a href="#lem:filtered-hecke-summands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:filtered-hecke-summands">27</a> makes its filtration pure of index $t_\eta$.

It remains to relate this filtered rank to the Hodge type of the same complex generalized space. Put $$V_{\mathrm{dR}}=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(A_S/K).$$ Its filtration and Hecke operators are defined over the fixed field $K$. For a numerical character $\chi$, choose a polynomial expression for $p_\chi$, and let $k_\chi\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ be the actual compositum of $K$ and its algebraic coefficients. Evaluating that polynomial in the fixed operators gives an idempotent on $V_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_K k_\chi$. Its induced filtration is its projected filtration, since the idempotent preserves $F^\bullet$. The dimensions of its image and of this filtration are ranks of matrices over $k_\chi$; they are unchanged under the restriction of the fixed embedding $\iota$ to $k_\chi$.

Over the given embedding $K\subset\mathbb{C}$, Betti–de Rham comparison intertwines the same operators and polynomial. It identifies these ranks with the filtration ranks on $H_\chi\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}$. For a scalar-conjugate numerical character $\sigma\chi$, repeat this argument with $p_{\sigma\chi}$ in the same $K$-defined operators. On Betti cohomology their rational matrices give $\sigma H_\chi=H_{\sigma\chi}$. We have therefore computed the type of the scalar-conjugated Betti space on the same complex variety. Each conjugate projector is evaluated separately in the fixed $K$-defined operators. This works even when $K$ intersects the character field, since it uses ranks over their actual compositum.

Finally the complex Hecke operators preserve both $H^{1,0}$ and $H^{0,1}$. Hence a full projected $F^1$ means that the whole generalized space is holomorphic, and zero projected $F^1$ means that it is antiholomorphic. Equation <a href="#eq:active-hodge-slope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-hodge-slope">[eq:active-hodge-slope]</a> depends only on $\eta$, so summing all full extensions gives the assertion for $H^{\mathrm{act}}_\eta$. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:rational-cm-sources"></a>
#### Rational CM sources for the theta classes

The preceding proposition determines the type of every vector in each scalar-conjugate active Hecke space. We can therefore apply scalar descent to maps with arbitrary algebraic Betti vectors in these spaces. This will prove the rational-map span asserted in Theorem <a href="#thm:cm-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm-extraction">18</a>.

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-active-hodge-types-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-active-hodge-types</summary>

<a id="eq:cm-sign-to-hodge-type"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:cm-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm-extraction">18</a>.* Use the Albanese identification $H^1(A_S,\mathbb{Q})\simeq H^1(S,\mathbb{Q})$. Extend a theta class from $S_0$ by zero on the other components of $S$. This is compatible with the active algebra, because Lemma <a href="#lem:active-hecke-theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:active-hecke-theta">23</a> makes that algebra component-preserving. The class is holomorphic by Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-one" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-one">15</a>. Decompose it into the active generalized character spaces of Equation <a href="#eq:active-generalized-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-generalized-space">[eq:active-generalized-space]</a>. For each nonzero component, let $\eta$ be its active character and $e$ its parameter from Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-satake" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-satake">25</a>. For every $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})$, put $g=\sigma|_E$. That proposition and Proposition <a href="#prop:active-hodge-types" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:active-hodge-types">28</a> give $$\label{eq:cm-sign-to-hodge-type}
\nu_\iota(\sigma e)=-\frac32v(g),\qquad
t_{\sigma\eta}
=\frac12-\frac13\nu_\iota(\sigma e)
=\frac{1+v(g)}2.$$ The possible sign in the conjugate parameter $e'=\pm\sigma e$ has valuation zero. Consequently the entire active space for $\sigma\eta$ is of type $(1,0)$ when $v(g)=1$ and of type $(0,1)$ when $v(g)=-1$. This applies to all of its vectors, not just to theta vectors. At the identity it gives type $(1,0)$, in agreement with $v(1)=1$ and the holomorphy of the theta class.

Each active space is defined over $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ in the rational Betti structure, because its idempotent has algebraic coefficients. Choose $0\ne x\in H^{\mathrm{act}}_\eta$ and $0\ne u\in U(v)_1(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})$. Define a $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$-linear map $$h:U(v)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\longrightarrow H^1(S,\overline{\mathbb{Q}})$$ by $h(u)=x$ and by setting it equal to zero on every other embedding line of $U(v)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}$. This is an element of $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(U(v),H^1(S,\mathbb{Q})\bigr)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}.$$ Scalar conjugation by $\sigma$ moves its nonzero source line to $U(v)_g$ and its target vector to $\sigma x\in H^{\mathrm{act}}_{\sigma\eta}$. The source line has the type prescribed by $v(g)$, and Equation <a href="#eq:cm-sign-to-hodge-type" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-sign-to-hodge-type">[eq:cm-sign-to-hodge-type]</a> gives the same type for the target. All other source lines map to zero. Hence every scalar conjugate of $h$ is a Hodge map.

Lemma <a href="#lem:trace-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trace-descent">2</a>, applied to the complex-linear subspace of Hodge maps, expresses $h$ as a $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$-linear combination of rational Hodge maps. Algebraic-Betti vectors span each complex active space, so taking complex spans covers every active component of the original theta class, including classes with transcendental coefficients. Restriction from $S$ to $S_0$ is a rational Hodge map and gives Equation <a href="#eq:cm-extraction-span" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-extraction-span">[eq:cm-extraction-span]</a>.

For a finite collection of inputs, all level choices are made before the auxiliary prime is chosen, and only finitely many primes are excluded. The preceding argument therefore applies to that collection at one sufficiently deep level. If $\pi:S'_0\to S_0$ is a deeper principal cover, then $\pi^*$ is a rational Hodge map and the theta classes pull back to their classes on $S'_0$. Applying $\pi^*$ to Equation <a href="#eq:cm-extraction-span" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-extraction-span">[eq:cm-extraction-span]</a> proves persistence. ◻

</details>

By Lemma <a href="#lem:weight-one-maps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weight-one-maps">3</a>, each rational map in Equation <a href="#eq:cm-extraction-span" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-extraction-span">[eq:cm-extraction-span]</a> is, up to a rational multiple, pullback along a morphism $S_0\to B(v)$.

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-family-extraction" class="corollary">

**Corollary 29** (Finite-family CM extraction). *Fix $E,V,D,\Lambda$ and the principal lattice levels of Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">5</a>. Let $v_1,\ldots,v_m:G_E\to\{1,-1\}$ be odd with $v_j(1)=1$. For each $j$, choose a finite family of finite adelic Schwartz inputs in the multiplicity-line construction $d_j(\tau)=v_j(\tau^{-1})$. Let $X_{\mathrm{base}}$ be a principal quotient at a torsion-free level in this framework. There is a common deeper principal cover $Y\to X_{\mathrm{base}}$, with $Y$ a smooth connected projective surface, on which every associated theta one-form class from the $j$-th family belongs to $$\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{
f\bigl(U(v_j)_1\bigr):
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\bigl(U(v_j),H^1(Y,\mathbb{Q})\bigr)
\right\}.$$ The maps are rational Hodge maps, with their images viewed after extension to $\mathbb{C}$. The conclusion persists after pullback to every deeper principal level. The auxiliary primes used for different $v_j$ may differ.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:cm-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm-extraction">18</a> to each finite input family. Choose a principal level contained in the resulting finitely many principal levels and in the level of $X_{\mathrm{base}}$, and deepen it until Lemma <a href="#lem:reference-moduli" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:reference-moduli">19</a> applies. Its quotient $Y$ is smooth, connected, and projective. The reference moduli construction depends on $V$, not on the multiplicity line, so it applies to this common level. The persistence in Theorem <a href="#thm:cm-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm-extraction">18</a> pulls all the rational-map spans to $Y$ and then to every deeper principal level. Each application chooses its auxiliary prime only after its own finite family and level have been fixed; the primes need not agree. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:assembly"></a>
### Completion of the CM proof

The theta construction gives a nonzero period on a compact ball quotient. The finite-family extraction corollary expresses the one-form classes in that period through rational maps from the prescribed CM modules, after passage to sufficiently deep levels. We put these statements on one projective surface and apply the criterion of Section <a href="#sec:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:surface">3</a>.

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:switch">6</a>.* By Lemma <a href="#lem:switch-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:switch-normalization">8</a>, it suffices to treat the case $s\geq2$ and $v_i(1)=1$ for all four functions. Use the common Galois CM field $E$, the active embedding $\mu=1$, and the line signs $$d_i(\tau)=v_i(\tau^{-1}).$$ They satisfy $d_1+d_2=d_3+d_4$ and $d_i(\mu)=1$. Choose the rank-three Hermitian space $V$, the ball $D$, and the rational orthogonal decomposition $W=W_1\perp W_2$ prescribed by $d_1,d_2$, as in Section <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a>.

Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-top">16</a>(ii) supplies a total finite theta input whose form for this decomposition is nonzero. With that input fixed, Corollary <a href="#cor:theta-switch-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:theta-switch-continuity">17</a> supplies a second rational orthogonal decomposition and a nonzero mixed period. Write $X'$ for the individual-factor cover constructed there, and $\xi_1,\xi_2$ for the two pulled-back endpoint forms. On this common compact principal quotient they have finite expansions $$\xi_1=\sum_{a\in I}c_a\,
       \Theta_1(\phi_{1a})\wedge\Theta_2(\phi_{2a}),
 \qquad
 \xi_2=\sum_{b\in J}c'_b\,
       \Theta_3(\phi_{3b})\wedge\Theta_4(\phi_{4b}),$$ where the constants account for the chosen constant-line trivializations, and $$\int_{X'}\xi_1\wedge\overline{\xi_2}\ne0.$$ All four families of individual inputs here are finite. The quotient and these expansions may be pulled to any deeper principal level; the integral is then multiplied by the degree of the cover.

Apply Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-family-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-family-extraction">29</a> to these four families, with $X_{\mathrm{base}}=X'$ and the fixed $E,V,D,\Lambda$. It gives a common smooth connected projective principal cover $\pi:Y\to X'$ on which all four $U(v_i)_1$-span conclusions hold; the auxiliary primes may differ.

<a id="eq:assembly-period"></a>
Denote the pulled-back one-form classes by $$\vartheta_{ia}=[\pi^*\Theta_i(\phi_{ia})]\in H^1(Y,\mathbb{C}),$$ with $a\in I$ for $i=1,2$ and $a\in J$ for $i=3,4$. These classes are represented by holomorphic one-forms. Their cup products compute the integrals of the wedge products, and the covering formula gives $$\label{eq:assembly-period}
\begin{split}
 0&\ne\int_Y\pi^*\xi_1\wedge\overline{\pi^*\xi_2}\\
  &=\sum_{a\in I,\ b\in J}c_a\overline{c'_b}\,
    \left\langle
      \vartheta_{1a}\cup\vartheta_{2a}\cup
      \overline{\vartheta_{3b}}\cup\overline{\vartheta_{4b}},[Y]
    \right\rangle .
\end{split}$$ Choose $a,b$ for which the displayed cup-product period is nonzero, and write its four holomorphic factors as $\vartheta_i$.

Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-family-extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-family-extraction">29</a> gives $$\vartheta_i\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}
 \{h(U(v_i)_1):
       h\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(U(v_i),H^1(Y,\mathbb{Q}))\},
 \qquad 1\leq i\leq4.$$ The maps are rational, so they commute with scalar complex conjugation, which sends $U(v_i)_1$ to $U(v_i)_c$. Consequently $$\overline{\vartheta_i}\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}
 \{h(U(v_i)_c):
       h\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(U(v_i),H^1(Y,\mathbb{Q}))\},
 \qquad i=3,4.$$ The four classes $\vartheta_1,\vartheta_2,\overline{\vartheta_3},
\overline{\vartheta_4}$ thus satisfy Corollary <a href="#cor:surface-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:surface-span">10</a> on the same projective surface $Y$, with the source labels $(1,1,c,c)$ and the nonzero period selected from Equation <a href="#eq:assembly-period" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:assembly-period">[eq:assembly-period]</a>. The normalized switch line is therefore algebraic. Lemma <a href="#lem:switch-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:switch-normalization">8</a> returns every switch to this case or to a product of algebraic two-factor classes, and proves the theorem. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-family-extraction</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a>.* The four-factor switch theorem is now proved for every Galois CM field. Proposition <a href="#prop:switch-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:switch-reduction">7</a> consequently places every rational Hodge class on a CM abelian variety in its rational cycle span. Its final scalar descent takes place on the original variety. Finite products and powers are CM, so the same conclusion covers them. ◻

</details>

<a id="cc:section"></a>
### Generalized Hodge and arithmetic consequences

Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a> applies to products of CM abelian varieties and therefore algebraizes rational Hodge correspondences between them. We use this to obtain the algebraic supports predicted by the generalized Hodge conjecture, following Abdulali’s effective-realization argument (Abdulali 2005, Lemma 1 and Theorems 3–4) <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[Abdulali2005CM](#ref-Abdulali2005CM)]. Hazama had earlier reduced this implication to codimension-two Hodge classes on a special family of CM abelian varieties (Hazama 2003, Theorem 8.3) <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[Hazama2003CM](#ref-Hazama2003CM)]. We then apply Milne’s Hodge-to-Tate and polarization theorems to obtain the Tate and Hodge standard conjectures for abelian varieties. A final application uses CM transport to algebraize specialized Hodge classes on arbitrary good-reduction abelian varieties.

<a id="pm-h-30"></a>
#### The generalized Hodge conjecture

A pure rational Hodge structure is *effective* if all its nonzero Hodge components have nonnegative indices. Recall that $V(r)^{p,q}=V^{p+r,q+r}$, so the twist $V(r)$ has weight $\operatorname{wt}(V)-2r$. The Hodge circle of a pure Hodge structure $V$ acts on $V^{p,q}$ by $z^{p-q}$, for $|z|=1$. Let $J(V)$ be the smallest algebraic subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V)$ over $\mathbb{Q}$ whose real points contain that circle. A polarizable $V$ is *of CM type* if $J(V)$ is a torus, with the trivial group allowed. Every Hodge substructure of the cohomology of a CM abelian variety is of CM type: its Hodge-circle group is an image of the torus acting on degree-one cohomology.

For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the geometric coniveau filtration is $$N^rH^k(X,\mathbb{Q})=
 \sum_{\substack{Z\subset X\text{ closed}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_X Z\ge r}}
 \ker\bigl(H^k(X,\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow H^k(X\setminus Z,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$ Grothendieck’s corrected generalized Hodge conjecture says that a rational Hodge substructure $W\subset H^k(X,\mathbb{Q})$ lies in $N^rH^k(X,\mathbb{Q})$ whenever $W(r)$ is effective; see (Grothendieck 1969, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[Grothendieck1969General](#ref-Grothendieck1969General)] and (Hazama 2003, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[Hazama2003CM](#ref-Hazama2003CM)]. Equivalently, one closed subset of codimension at least $r$ supports all of $W$. Indeed, finite dimensionality permits taking a finite union of the supports needed for a basis of $W$.

We will obtain such a support by algebraizing a Hodge map from an auxiliary CM abelian variety. To get the required codimension, that variety must realize an effective twist of $W$ in cohomology of the same weight; the twisting index will determine the codimension of the resulting correspondence. The next lemma constructs it by expressing the Hodge indices at each embedding as sums of weight-one indices.

<a id="stmt-cc-realization"></a>
<div id="cc:realization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 30** (Effective realization of a simple CM Hodge structure). *Let $W$ be a nonzero simple polarizable rational Hodge structure of CM type and weight $k$. Put $$r_0=\min\{p:W^{p,k-p}\ne0\},\qquad n=k-2r_0.$$ Then $n\ge0$, and there is a CM abelian variety $B$, possibly a point, and a surjective rational Hodge map $$H^n(B,\mathbb{Q})(-r_0)\longrightarrow W.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cc-realization"><summary>Proof of stmt-cc-realization</summary>

*Proof.* Set $U=W(r_0)$. Hodge symmetry shows that $U$ is effective of weight $n\ge0$, with smallest Hodge index zero. If $n=0$, every type of $U$ is $(0,0)$. Simplicity then gives $U\simeq\mathbb{Q}$, and we take $B$ to be a point.

Suppose $n>0$. We first show that $U$ is a line over a CM field; we will then decompose its Hodge type into weight-one CM types. Write $T=J(U)$. Let $E_0$ be the $\mathbb{Q}$-subalgebra of $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_\mathbb{Q}(U)$ generated by the image of $T$. After splitting the torus, its action is diagonal by characters, so $E_0$ is a commutative finite étale algebra. Its field idempotents and every subspace linear over one field factor are $T$-stable. Simplicity of $U$ therefore forces $E_0$ to be a field $E$ and $\dim_E U=1$.

We check that $E$ is a CM field. Let the Weil operator $C_U$ act as $i^{p-q}$ on $U^{p,q}$. By polarizability there is a rational Hodge pairing $\psi$, with the conventional weight-dependent sign absorbed in its definition, for which $$q(x,y)=\psi(C_Ux,y)$$ is positive definite on $U_\mathbb{R}$. The adjoint $\dagger$ for $\psi$ sends $t\in T$ to $t^{-1}$, hence preserves $E$. Every element of $E$ commutes with $C_U$, so $\dagger$ is also the adjoint for $q$. On the real finite étale algebra $E_\mathbb{R}$, it must fix each primitive idempotent: if an idempotent $e$ were sent to a different one, then $e^\dagger e=0$, contrary to $q(ex,ex)=q(x,e^\dagger ex)>0$ for some $x$. On a complex factor it must act by complex conjugation, since the identity would give $i^\dagger i=-1$, again contrary to positivity. Thus if $\dagger$ is trivial on $E$, the field is totally real. If it is nontrivial, a real embedding would force $\dagger$ to be trivial on $E$, so there are no real embeddings; the fixed algebra in $E_\mathbb{R}$ is then a product of copies of $\mathbb{R}$. Its fixed field is therefore totally real, and $E$ is CM.

The totally real case cannot occur here. The Hodge circle acts through $E_\mathbb{R}^\times$; its image in each real scalar factor is a compact connected subgroup of $\mathbb{R}^\times$, hence is trivial. All Hodge types of $U$ would then have equal indices. Since their smallest index is zero, this would force $n=0$. Consequently $E$ is CM, and $\dagger$ is its complex conjugation.

Let $\Sigma=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,\mathbb{C})$, and write $\bar\sigma$ for the conjugate of $\sigma$. Since $E$ acts by Hodge endomorphisms and $U$ is an $E$-line, $$U_\mathbb{C}=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma}U_\sigma,\qquad
 U_\sigma\text{ has type }(a_\sigma,n-a_\sigma),$$ where $0\le a_\sigma\le n$ and $a_\sigma+a_{\bar\sigma}=n$. Choose $n$ CM types $\Phi_1,\ldots,\Phi_n$ of $E$ with $$\#\{j:\sigma\in\Phi_j\}=a_\sigma.$$ This is possible independently for each conjugate pair: put $\sigma$ into any $a_\sigma$ lists and $\bar\sigma$ into the remaining lists.

For each $j$, give the rational vector space $H_j=E$ the weight-one Hodge structure whose $\sigma$-line has type $(1,0)$ exactly when $\sigma\in\Phi_j$. The polarization and Riemann construction of Section <a href="#sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensors">2</a>, surrounding Equation <a href="#eq:cm-types" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-types">[eq:cm-types]</a>, applies to any CM field, with no Galois hypothesis. It gives a CM abelian variety $B_j$ with $H^1(B_j,\mathbb{Q})\simeq H_j$.

Identify $U$ and the $H_j$ with $E$ as $E$-lines. The rational multiplication map $$E^{\otimes_\mathbb{Q}n}\longrightarrow E,\qquad
 x_1\otimes\cdots\otimes x_n\longmapsto x_1\cdots x_n$$ is surjective. Over $\mathbb{C}$ it kills tensors with unequal embedding labels, while the tensor with all labels equal to $\sigma$ has type $(a_\sigma,n-a_\sigma)$ and maps to $U_\sigma$. It is therefore a Hodge map to $U$. For $B=\prod_{j=1}^nB_j$, its source is the rational Künneth summand $\bigotimes_j H^1(B_j,\mathbb{Q})$ of $H^n(B,\mathbb{Q})$. Extending by zero on the other summands and twisting by $(-r_0)$ proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cc-ghc"></a>
<div id="cc:ghc" class="corollary">

**Corollary 31** (Generalized Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties). *Let $A$ be a complex CM abelian variety. For integers $k,r\ge0$ and a rational Hodge substructure $W\subset H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})$ such that $W(r)$ is effective, there is a closed algebraic subset $Z\subset A$ of codimension at least $r$ such that $$W\subset\ker\bigl(H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow
                         H^k(A\setminus Z,\mathbb{Q})\bigr).$$ This includes every finite product of CM abelian varieties and every power of such a product.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cc-ghc"><summary>Proof of stmt-cc-ghc</summary>

*Proof.* The zero substructure permits $Z=\varnothing$. Otherwise, semisimplicity of polarizable rational Hodge structures reduces the claim to a simple summand $W$, after which we take the finite union of the resulting supports. Let $r_0$ be its smallest Hodge index. Then $r_0\ge r$. Also $r_0\le k/2\le\dim A$, since $W\ne0$. Put $n=k-2r_0$, take $B$ from Lemma <a href="#cc:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cc:realization">30</a>, and write $b=\dim B$. Composing the map of that lemma with $W\hookrightarrow H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})$ gives $$f:H^n(B,\mathbb{Q})(-r_0)\longrightarrow H^k(A,\mathbb{Q}),
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{im}}(f)=W.$$ By Poincaré duality, $f$ corresponds to a rational Hodge class of type $(b+r_0,b+r_0)$ in $$H^{2b-n}(B,\mathbb{Q})\otimes H^k(A,\mathbb{Q})
 \subset H^{2b+2r_0}(B\times A,\mathbb{Q}).$$ The product $B\times A$ is CM. Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a> therefore represents this exact class by $\Gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^{b+r_0}(B\times A)_\mathbb{Q}$, whose action in degree $n$ is $f$.

Choose a finite rational cycle representing $\Gamma$, and let $Z$ be the projection of its support to $A$. Every component of that cycle has dimension $$\dim(B\times A)-(b+r_0)=\dim A-r_0.$$ The projection is proper, so $Z$ is closed and $\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_A Z\ge r_0\ge r$. The correspondence restricts to zero on $B\times(A\setminus Z)$; functoriality of its cohomological action therefore makes its image $W$ restrict to zero on $A\setminus Z$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-31"></a>
#### The Tate conjecture over finite fields

Let $A$ be an abelian variety over $\mathbb{F}_q$, put $\bar A=A\times_{\mathbb{F}_q}\overline{\mathbb{F}_q}$, and write $G_q=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_q}/\mathbb{F}_q)$. For a prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F}_q)$, the rational Tate conjecture in codimension $r$ asks whether cycles over $\mathbb{F}_q$ span $H^{2r}_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\bar A,\mathbb{Q}_\ell(r))^{G_q}$.

<a id="stmt-cc-tate"></a>
<div id="cc:tate" class="corollary">

**Corollary 32** (Tate conjecture for abelian varieties over finite fields). *For every abelian variety $A/\mathbb{F}_q$, every integer $r\ge0$, and every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F}_q)$, the cycle-class map $$\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^r(A)\otimes_\mathbb{Z}\mathbb{Q}_\ell\longrightarrow
 H^{2r}_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\bar A,\mathbb{Q}_\ell(r))^{G_q}$$ is surjective. Numerical equivalence and $\ell$-adic homological equivalence coincide on rational algebraic cycles on $\bar A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cc-tate"><summary>Proof of stmt-cc-tate</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Milne’s Theorem 7.1 and his formulation (0.1) (Milne 1999), with the correction in (Milne, n.d.a): the cofinal auxiliary Galois CM fields are enlarged to contain a real subfield of degree $>2$ inert at the residue prime. The theorem’s hypothesis is supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a>. It gives the stated equality of equivalence relations and algebraic spanning of the geometric Tate classes: those fixed over some finite field of definition.

Let $\alpha$ lie in the displayed $G_q$-invariant space. The geometric conclusion writes $\alpha$ as a finite $\mathbb{Q}_\ell$-linear combination of cycle classes. All the cycles in this expression descend to $A_{\mathbb F_{q^m}}$ for one $m$; let $z_m\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^r(A_{\mathbb F_{q^m}})\otimes_\mathbb{Z}\mathbb{Q}_\ell$ denote the resulting combination. For the finite projection $\pi_m:A_{\mathbb F_{q^m}}\to A$, proper pushforward and base change for cycle classes give $$\operatorname{cl}_\ell\!\left(\frac1m(\pi_m)_*z_m\right)
 =\frac1m\sum_{\sigma\in
        \mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathbb F_{q^m}/\mathbb{F}_q)}\sigma(\alpha)=\alpha.$$ Thus $m^{-1}(\pi_m)_*z_m$ is the required class over $\mathbb{F}_q$. Division by $m$ is valid in $\mathbb{Q}_\ell$, even when $m$ is divisible by $\ell$ or by the characteristic. ◻

</details>

Milne’s implication compares CM and finite-field abelian motives. It does not assert that each Tate class lifts to a Hodge class on a single CM lifting, even up to isogeny (Milne 1999, Introduction, Remark (a)) <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[Milne1999Tate](#ref-Milne1999Tate)].

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### The Hodge standard conjecture

Let $A$ be an abelian variety of dimension $g$ over an algebraically closed field $K$, and let $A^r_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}$ be the space of rational codimension-$r$ cycles modulo numerical equivalence. A *rational ample divisor class* is an $h\in A^1_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}$ for which some positive integral multiple is the numerical class of an ample Cartier divisor. For integers $0\le r\le g/2$, put $$P_h^r(A)=\ker\!\left(
 h^{g-2r+1}:A^r_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}
 \longrightarrow A^{g-r+1}_{\mathrm{num}}(A)_\mathbb{Q}\right).$$

<a id="stmt-cc-standard"></a>
<div id="cc:standard" class="corollary">

**Corollary 33** (Hodge standard conjecture for abelian varieties). *Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field and $A/K$ an abelian variety of dimension $g$. For every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(K)$, numerical and $\ell$-adic homological equivalence coincide on rational algebraic cycles on $A$. For every rational ample divisor class $h$ and every integer $0\le r\le g/2$, the rational symmetric form $$P_h^r(A)\times P_h^r(A)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q},\qquad
 (x,y)\longmapsto(-1)^r\deg(xy\,h^{g-2r})$$ is positive definite after extension of scalars to $\mathbb{R}$. Thus the Hodge standard conjecture for $\ell$-adic étale cohomology holds for abelian varieties in arbitrary characteristic.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cc-standard"><summary>Proof of stmt-cc-standard</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Milne’s Theorem 3.3 (Milne 2002) <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[Milne2002Polarizations](#ref-Milne2002Polarizations)] using Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a>. In Milne’s proof, the same Hodge hypothesis supplies the compatibility and positivity of the relevant polarizations through Theorem 2.1, read with the author’s addendum (Milne, n.d.b) and the corrected auxiliary-field choice from (Milne, n.d.a). Theorem 3.3 gives both the equality of equivalence relations and positivity on the primitive space of cycles modulo homological equivalence. The former identifies that primitive space with the numerical one above.

The case $g=0$ is immediate. For $g>0$, choose a positive integer $m$ so that $mh$ is the class of a smooth hyperplane section; clearing denominators, taking a sufficiently large very ample multiple, and Bertini’s theorem permit this. Multiplication by the nonzero rational scalar $m^{g-2r+1}$ does not change the primitive kernel, and the form for $mh$ is $m^{g-2r}$ times the form for $h$. This positive factor preserves positive definiteness. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
#### Specialization of Hodge classes

<a id="stmt-cc-algebraic-specialization"></a>
<div id="cc:algebraic-specialization" class="remark">

**Remark 34** (Algebraicity of specialized Hodge classes). Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a> also combines with the CM transport of (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[OpenAIMilneRationality2026](#ref-OpenAIMilneRationality2026)] to give a specialization statement for abelian varieties that need not be CM. Fix a prime $p$ and a $p$-adic place of $\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$. If $A/\overline{\mathbb{Q}}$ has good reduction $A_0/\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ at that place, every rational Hodge class on $A$ specializes to the class of one rational algebraic cycle on $A_0$, simultaneously in all prime-to-$p$ realizations and in crystalline cohomology after extension to the completed field at the chosen place. Indeed, the transport writes that specialization as the pullback of a specialized CM Hodge class by a rational homomorphism of special fibers. Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a> algebraizes the CM class; specialization and Chow pullback after clearing the homomorphism’s denominator give the required cycle. See (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[OpenAIMilneRationality2026](#ref-OpenAIMilneRationality2026)] for the field-of-definition argument and precise realization conventions, including $p=2$. This application uses the CM transport, not the independent rationality proof of that paper, and is not an input to Theorem <a href="#thm:cm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cm">1</a>. It asserts algebraicity on the special fiber, not the Hodge conjecture for arbitrary complex abelian varieties.

</div>

[^1]: For the proper formal model and the algebraic/analytic cohomology identifications, see (Bosch 2014, vol. 2105, sec. 7.2, p. 161) and (Scholze 2013, Theorems 9.1 and 9.3), read with (Scholze 2016). Passage from the algebraic closure to its completion uses (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tag 0DDG). The étale comparisons are applied with $\mathbb{Z}/q^n$-coefficients, then passed to inverse limits and rationalized.

      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "Surjectivity of the cycle class map for complex multiplication abelian varieties is a foundational prerequisite for ensuring that all Hodge classes in complex multiplication supplied subspaces are algebraic.",
      "relation": "prerequisite",
      "source_anchor": "thm:cm",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-032.1",
      "source_quote": "rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on every finite product of complex CM abelian varieties",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-comp-tools",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-032.4",
      "target_quote": "Every rational Hodge class in a CM-supplied subspace is algebraic.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "Setting the curve power to zero in the product formulation specialises the mixed product theorem directly to the case of pure powers of complex multiplication varieties.",
      "relation": "specialises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-cm",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-032.1",
      "source_quote": "the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on every finite product of complex CM abelian varieties and on every power of such a product.",

      "target_pm_id": "PM-032.4",
